\displaystyle\mathrm{rank}\left(\{\overline{y}^{r(1)},\dots,\overline{y}^{r(p)}\}\right)\geq 6.

Therefore, the remainder of
\pi
with
Type
(T-i)
is the one such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{rank}\left(\{\overline{y}^{r(1)},\dots,\overline{y}^{r(p)}\}\right)=3.

(2.22)

We remark that (
2.22
) holds in the following cases.

\displaystyle\text{$p=2$ and $\pi$ belongs to {Type}\ref{item:T1}},

(
\Delta_{2}
)

\displaystyle\text{$p=3$ and $s_{1}<s_{2}<t_{2}<s_{3}<t_{3}<t_{1}$},

(
\Delta_{3}(\pi_{1})
)

\displaystyle\text{$p=3$ and $s_{2}<s_{1}<t_{2}<s_{3}<t_{3}<t_{1}$},

(
\Delta_{3}(\pi_{2})
)

\displaystyle\text{$p=4$ and $s_{1}<s_{2}<t_{2}<s_{3}<t_{3}<s_{4}<t_{4}<t_{1}$},

(
\Delta_{4}(\pi_{1})
)

\displaystyle\text{$p=4$ and $s_{2}<s_{1}<t_{2}<s_{3}<t_{3}<s_{4}<t_{4}<t_{1}$}.

(
\Delta_{4}(\pi_{2})
)

Furthermore, by taking time-reversal (see Definition
2.11
) (

⁢

Δ
3

(

π
2

)

) and (

⁢

Δ
4

(

π
2

)

) are reduced to the case where

\displaystyle\mathrm{rank}\left(\{\widetilde{\overline{y}}^{\widetilde{r}(1)},\dots,\widetilde{\overline{y}}^{\widetilde{r}(p)}\}\right)\geq 6.

Hence, the remaining cases are (

Δ
2

), (

⁢

Δ
3

(

π
1

)

) and (

⁢

Δ
4

(

π
1

)

).
The estimates for these cases are proved by Propositions

,
2.18
,
2.19
,
2.20
below, and this finishes then this proof.

Let

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}(\pi_{1}):=\{0<s_{1}<s_{2}<t_{2}<t_{1}\leq 1\},

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}(\pi_{2}):=\{0<s_{1}<s_{2}<t_{1}<t_{2}\leq 1\}.

Proposition 2.17
.

For any
\gamma\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right)
, there exists
C>0
such that for any
n\geq 1

\displaystyle\left|\int_{T_{\varepsilon_{n-1},\varepsilon_{n}}^{2}\cap\Delta_{2}(\pi_{1})}\text{\rm d}{\mathbf{s}}\text{\rm d}\mathbf{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\text{\rm d}\mathbf{y}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{2}\left(e^{i\left\langle y_{j},u_{1}h_{\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}}^{(j)}\right\rangle}-e^{i\left\langle y_{j},u_{2}h_{\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}}^{(j)}\right\rangle}\right)\right)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{3}|R_{j}|\left|\overline{y}^{j}\right|^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{2\gamma}\varepsilon_{n-1}^{\gamma}

(2.23)

Proof.

Take
\gamma\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
. We set
s=s_{2}-s_{1}=|R_{1}|
,
t=t_{2}-s_{2}=|R_{2}|
,
u=t_{1}-t_{2}=|R_{3}|
. Then, the left-hand side of (
2.23
) is dominated by

\displaystyle C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{2\gamma}\varepsilon_{n-1}^{2\gamma}\int_{0<s+u<\varepsilon_{n-1}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\int_{\varepsilon_{n}<t<\varepsilon_{n-1}}\text{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 113.81102pt\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}y|x|^{\gamma}|y|^{\gamma}\exp\left(-\frac{s+u}{2}|x|^{2}-\frac{t}{2}|x+y|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{2\gamma}\varepsilon_{n-1}^{2\gamma}\int_{0<s+u<\varepsilon_{n-1}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\int_{\varepsilon_{n}<t<\varepsilon_{n-1}}\text{\rm d}t\

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 113.81102pt\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}z|x|^{\gamma}(|x|^{\gamma}+|z|^{\gamma})\exp\left(-\frac{s+u}{2}|x|^{2}-\frac{t}{2}|z|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{2\gamma}\varepsilon_{n-1}^{\gamma}.

Here, note the choice of
\varepsilon_{n}
(see (
2.2
)).

Proposition 2.18
.

For any
\gamma\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right)
, there exists
C>0
such that for any
n\geq 1

\displaystyle\left|\int_{T_{\varepsilon_{n-1},e_{n}}^{2}\cap\Delta_{2}(\pi_{2})}\text{\rm d}{\mathbf{s}}\text{\rm d}\mathbf{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\text{\rm d}\mathbf{y}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{2}\left(e^{i\left\langle y_{j},u_{1}h_{\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}}^{(j)}\right\rangle}-e^{i\left\langle y_{j},u_{2}h_{\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}}^{(j)}\right\rangle}\right)\right)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{3}|R_{j}|\left|\overline{y}^{j}\right|^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{2\gamma}\varepsilon_{n-1}^{\gamma}.

(2.24)

Proof.

Take
\gamma\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
. We set
s=s_{2}-s_{1}=|R_{1}|
,
t=t_{1}-s_{2}=|R_{2}|
,
r=t_{2}-t_{1}=|R_{3}|
. Then, the left-hand side of (
2.23
) is dominated by

\displaystyle C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{2\gamma}\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\varepsilon_{n}<s+t<\varepsilon_{n-1}\\
\varepsilon_{n}<t+r<\varepsilon_{n-1}\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}t\text{\rm d}r

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 28.45274pt\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}y(s+t)^{\gamma}(t+r)^{\gamma}|x|^{\gamma}|y|^{\gamma}\exp\left(-\frac{s}{2}\left|x\right|^{2}-\frac{t}{2}|x+y|^{2}-\frac{r}{2}|y|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{2\gamma}\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\varepsilon_{n}<s+t<\varepsilon_{n-1}\\
\varepsilon_{n}<t+r<\varepsilon_{n-1}\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}t\text{\rm d}r

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}y(s+t)^{\gamma}(t+r)^{\gamma}|x|^{\gamma}|y|^{\gamma}\exp\left(-\frac{s+t}{2}\left|x+\frac{t}{s+t}y\right|^{2}-\frac{st+tr+rs}{2(s+t)}|y|^{2}\right).

Changing variables
x+\frac{t}{s+t}y\mapsto z
,
y\mapsto y
, we can get an upper bound of the integral,

\displaystyle\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\varepsilon_{n}<s+t<\varepsilon_{n-1}\\
\varepsilon_{n}<t+r<\varepsilon_{n-1}\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}t\text{\rm d}r(s+t)^{\gamma}(t+r)^{\gamma}(st+tr+rs)^{-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}

\displaystyle+\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\varepsilon_{n}<s+t<\varepsilon_{n-1}\\
\varepsilon_{n}<t+r<\varepsilon_{n-1}\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}t\text{\rm d}r(s+t)^{\gamma}t^{\gamma}(t+r)^{\gamma}(st+tr+rs)^{-\frac{3+2\gamma}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{n-1}^{2\gamma}\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\varepsilon_{n}<s+t<\varepsilon_{n-1}\\
\varepsilon_{n}<t+r<\varepsilon_{n-1}\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}t\text{\rm d}r(st+tr+rs)^{-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon_{n-1}^{3\gamma}\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\varepsilon_{n}<s+t<\varepsilon_{n-1}\\
\varepsilon_{n}<t+r<\varepsilon_{n-1}\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}t\text{\rm d}r(st+tr+rs)^{-\frac{3+2\gamma}{2}}.

These terms are estimated by
C\varepsilon_{n-1}^{\gamma}
, because for
a\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\varepsilon_{n}<s+t<\varepsilon_{n-1}\\
\varepsilon_{n}<t+r<\varepsilon_{n-1}\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}t\text{\rm d}r(st+tr+rs)^{-\frac{3}{2}-a}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\varepsilon_{n}<s+t<\varepsilon_{n-1}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}t(s+t)^{-1}(st)^{-\frac{1}{2}-a}

\displaystyle=C\int_{\varepsilon_{n}<R<\varepsilon_{n-1},0<s<R}\text{\rm d}R\text{\rm d}s\left[R^{-1}(s(R-s))^{-\frac{1}{2}-a}\right]

\displaystyle=CB\left(\frac{1}{2}-a,\frac{1}{2}-a\right)\int_{\varepsilon_{n}<R<\varepsilon_{n-1}}\text{\rm d}RR^{-1-2a}\leq C\varepsilon_{n}^{-2a}.

where
B(a,b)=\int_{0}^{1}x^{a-1}(1-x)^{b-1}\text{\rm d}x
is the Bessel function for
a,b>0
.
Hence, we have the assertion.

We can prove the following propositions by the same argument as in Proposition
2.17
.

Proposition 2.19
.

For
\gamma\in(0,1)
, there exists
C>0
such that for any
n\geq 1

\displaystyle\left|\int_{T_{\varepsilon_{n-1},e_{n}}^{3}\cap\Delta_{3}(\pi_{1})}\text{\rm d}{\mathbf{s}}\text{\rm d}\mathbf{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{9}}\text{\rm d}\mathbf{y}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{3}\left(e^{i\left\langle y_{j},u_{1}h_{\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}}^{(j)}\right\rangle}-e^{i\left\langle y_{j},u_{2}h_{\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}}^{(j)}\right\rangle}\right)\right)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{5}|R_{j}|\left|\overline{y}^{j}\right|^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{3\gamma}\varepsilon_{n}^{\frac{1+3\gamma}{2}}.

(2.25)

Proof.

Take
\gamma\in(0,1)
. We set
r_{1}=s_{2}-s_{1}
,
s=t_{2}-s_{2}
,
r_{2}=s_{3}-t_{2}
,
t=t_{3}-s_{3}
,
r_{3}=t_{1}-t_{1}
. Then, the left-hand side of (
2.25
) is dominated by

\displaystyle C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{3\gamma}\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}s,t\in[\varepsilon_{n},\varepsilon_{n-1}]\\
0<r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}<\varepsilon_{n-1}\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}t\text{\rm d}\mathbf{r}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{9}}\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}y\text{\rm d}z(s+t+r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3})^{\gamma}s^{\gamma}t^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 56.9055pt\times|x|^{\gamma}|y|^{\gamma}|z|^{\gamma}\exp\left(-\frac{s}{2}|x+z|^{2}-\frac{t}{2}|y+z|^{2}-\frac{r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}}{2}|z|^{2}\right).

Changing variables
x+z\mapsto\widetilde{x}
,
y+z\mapsto\widetilde{y}
, we can get an upper bound of the integral,

\displaystyle\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}s,t\in[\varepsilon_{n},\varepsilon_{n-1}]\\
0<r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}<\varepsilon_{n-1}\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}t\text{\rm d}\mathbf{r}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{9}}\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}y\text{\rm d}z(s+t+r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3})^{\gamma}s^{\gamma}t^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 56.9055pt\times|x|^{\gamma}|y|^{\gamma}|z|^{\gamma}\exp\left(-\frac{s}{2}|x+z|^{2}-\frac{t}{2}|y+z|^{2}-\frac{r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}}{2}|z|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}s,t\in[\varepsilon_{n},\varepsilon_{n-1}]\\
0<r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}<\varepsilon_{n-1}\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}t\text{\rm d}\mathbf{r}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{9}}\text{\rm d}\widetilde{x}\text{\rm d}\widetilde{y}\text{\rm d}z(s+t+r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3})^{\gamma}s^{\gamma}t^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 56.9055pt\times(|\widetilde{x}|^{\gamma}+|z|^{\gamma})(|y|^{\gamma}+|z|^{\gamma})|z|^{\gamma}\exp\left(-\frac{s}{2}|\widetilde{x}|^{2}-\frac{t}{2}|\widetilde{y}|^{2}-\frac{r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}}{2}|z|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon_{n-1}^{3\gamma}\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}s,t\in[\varepsilon_{n},\varepsilon_{n-1}]\\
0<r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}<\varepsilon_{n-1}\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}t\text{\rm d}\mathbf{r}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{9}}\text{\rm d}\widetilde{x}\text{\rm d}\widetilde{y}\text{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt(|\widetilde{x}|^{\gamma}+|z|^{\gamma})(|y|^{\gamma}+|z|^{\gamma})|z|^{\gamma}\exp\left(-\frac{s}{2}|\widetilde{x}|^{2}-\frac{t}{2}|\widetilde{y}|^{2}-\frac{r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}}{2}|z|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon_{n}^{\frac{1+3\gamma}{2}}.

Here, note the choice of
\varepsilon_{n}
(see (
2.2
)).

Proposition 2.20
.

There exists
C>0
such that for any
n\geq 1

\displaystyle\left|\int_{T_{\varepsilon_{n-1},e_{n}}^{4}\cap\Delta_{4}(\pi_{1})}\text{\rm d}{\mathbf{s}}\text{\rm d}\mathbf{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{12}}\text{\rm d}\mathbf{y}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{4}\left(e^{i\left\langle y_{j},u_{1}h_{\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}}^{(j)}\right\rangle}-e^{i\left\langle y_{j},u_{2}h_{\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}}^{(j)}\right\rangle}\right)\right)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{7}|R_{j}|\left|\overline{y}^{j}\right|^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{4}\varepsilon_{n}^{3}.

(2.26)

Proof.

We set
v_{1}=s_{2}-s_{1}
,
s=t_{2}-s_{2}
,
v_{2}=s_{3}-t_{2}
,
t=t_{3}-s_{3}
,
v_{3}=s_{4}-t_{3}
,
r=t_{4}-s_{4}
,
v_{4}=t_{1}-t_{4}
. Then, the lefthand side of (
2.26
) is dominated by

\displaystyle C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{4}\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}s,t,r\in[\varepsilon_{n},\varepsilon_{n-1}]\\
0<v_{1}+v_{2}+v_{3}+v_{4}<\varepsilon_{n-1}\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}t\text{\rm d}r\text{\rm d}\mathbf{v}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{12}}\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}y\text{\rm d}z\text{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt(s+t+r+v_{1}+v_{2}+v_{3}+v_{4})str|x||y||z||w|

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\times\exp\left(-\frac{s}{2}|x+w|^{2}-\frac{t}{2}|y+w|^{2}-\frac{r}{2}|z+w|^{2}-\frac{v_{1}+v_{2}+v_{3}+v_{4}}{2}|z|^{2}\right).

In a similar fashion to the discussion in Proposition
2.19
, we can then prove the statement of Proposition
2.20
.

2.4
Remarks on the treatment of Dirac functions

In this section, we will look at the self-intersection local time for pinned Brownian motion. In particular, its continuity will allow us to use the notation

\displaystyle E\left[e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\Phi\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{x_{i}}(\omega_{t_{i}})\right]

(see Remark
2.24
) where
\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}
is given after (
1.7
), which is useful in our analysis.

Similarly to
[
46
]
, we define a self-intersection local time of conditioned Brownian motion.
Fix
\varepsilon>0
. For each
0<u<v<1
,
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
,
a>0
, we define

\displaystyle H_{u,v,x,y}^{\varepsilon,a}:=\int_{\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}t\int_{0}^{t-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}sp_{a}\left(\Psi_{u,v,x,y}(\omega)(t)-\Psi_{u,v,x,y}(\omega)(s)\right),

where for
\omega\in C([0,1],{\mathbb{R}}^{3})

\displaystyle\Psi_{u,v,x,y}(\omega)(\tau)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle x\frac{\tau}{u}+\omega_{\tau}-\frac{\tau}{u}\omega_{u},\quad&0\leq\tau\leq u\\
\displaystyle x\left(1-\frac{\tau-u}{v-u}\right)+y\frac{\tau-u}{v-u}+\left(\omega_{\tau}-\omega_{u}-\frac{\tau-u}{v-u}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})\right),\quad&u\leq\tau\leq v\\
\displaystyle y+\omega_{\tau}-\omega_{v},\quad&v\leq\tau.\end{cases}

By modifying the proof of Proposition
2.14
or
[
46
, Proposition 2.1]
, we can prove the following.

Lemma 2.21
.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
and
0\leq u<v\leq 1
. Then, there exists a version of
H_{u,v,x,y}^{\varepsilon,a}
which is jointly continuous in
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, and
a\geq 0
. Moreover, the limit as
\searrow 0
denoted by
H_{u,v,x,y}^{\varepsilon}
exists and also has these properties.

Proof.

We omit the proof since it follows by the arguments of
[
46
, Proposition 2.1]
.
In fact we have the estimate that there exists
\gamma\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
such that for any
p\geq 2
,

\displaystyle E\left[\left|H_{u,v,x,y}^{\varepsilon,a}-H_{u,v,x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}^{\varepsilon,a^{\prime}}\right|^{p}\right]\leq C\left(|x-x^{\prime}|^{\gamma p}+|y-y^{\prime}|^{\gamma p}+|a-a^{\prime}|^{\frac{\gamma p}{2}}\right).

Remark 2.22
.

The map
\Psi_{u,v,x,y}
maps a Brownian motion to conditional Brownian motion which bridges between
(0,0)
,
(u,x)
,
(v,y)
. Lemma
2.21
can be generalized for any
n
intermediate points. For
0\leq t_{1}<\dots<t_{n}\leq 1
,
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, we denote by
H^{\varepsilon,a}_{(t_{i},x_{i})}
a modified version of
H
. We omit the upper index
a
for
a=0
.

Corollary 2.23
.

Let
\Phi
be any bounded measurable function. Then, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{a\to 0}E\left[e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\Phi\prod_{i=1}^{n}p_{a}(\omega_{t_{i}},x_{i})\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[\left.e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\Phi\right|\omega_{t_{i}}=x_{i},i=1,\dots,n\right]p_{t_{1}}(0,x_{1})\prod_{j=2}^{n}p_{t_{j}-t_{j-1}}(x_{j-1},x_{j})

for
0\leq t_{1}<\dots<t_{n}\leq 1
,
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
.

Proof.

From construction, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3n}}\text{\rm d}\mathbf{y}E\left[\left.e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\Phi\prod_{i=1}^{n}p_{a}(\omega_{t_{i}},x_{i})\right|\omega_{t_{i}}=y_{i},i=1,\dots,n\right]p_{t_{1}}(0,y_{1})\prod_{j=2}^{n}p_{t_{j}-t_{j-1}}(y_{j-1},y_{j})

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3n}}\text{\rm d}\mathbf{y}E\left[e^{-\lambda{H}^{\varepsilon}_{(t_{i},y_{i})}+\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)-\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)}\Phi\right]\prod_{i=1}^{n}p_{a}(y_{i},x_{i})p_{t_{1}}(0,y_{1})\prod_{j=2}^{n}p_{t_{j}-t_{j-1}}(y_{j-1},y_{j}).

Letting
a\searrow 0
, we see that the statement holds.

Corollary
2.23
allows us to use the formal expression in Remark
2.24
below.

Remark 2.24
.

Let us use the following notation:

\displaystyle E\left[e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\Phi\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{x_{i}}(\omega_{t_{i}})\right]

\displaystyle:=E\left[\left.e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\Phi\right|\omega_{t_{i}}=x_{i},i=1,\dots,n\right]p_{t_{1}}(0,x_{1})\prod_{j=2}^{n}p_{t_{j}-t_{j-1}}(x_{j-1},x_{j}).

Also, we set

\displaystyle E\left[e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\Phi\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s_{i}}-\omega_{t_{i}})\right]:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3n}}E\left[e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\Phi\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\delta_{x_{i}}(\omega_{s_{i}})\delta_{x_{i}}(\omega_{t_{i}})\right)\right]\text{\rm d}x_{1}\dots\text{\rm d}x_{n}

for
s_{1},\dots,s_{n},t_{1},\dots,t_{n}\in[0,1]
.

Similarly to Corollary
2.23
, we can show that

\displaystyle E\left[e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{x_{i}}(\omega_{r_{i}})\prod_{j=1}^{m}J_{s_{j},t_{j}}^{\varepsilon}\Phi\right]=\int_{T_{\varepsilon}^{m}}\text{\rm d}\mathbf{s}\text{\rm d}\mathbf{t}E\left[e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{x_{i}}(\omega_{r_{i}})\prod_{j=1}^{m}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s_{j}}-\omega_{t_{j}})\Phi\right]

for
r_{1},\dots,r_{n}\in[0,1]
and
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
. We omit the detail of the proof.

2.5
Differentiability

In this subsection, we will look at the differentiability of

\displaystyle\rho(\varepsilon):=E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right)\Phi\right]

in
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, where
\Phi
is any bounded random variable.

Lemma 2.25
.

\rho
is differentiable at
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
for any bounded random variable
\Phi
and satifies

\displaystyle\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\rho(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\lambda\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}sE\left[e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\Phi\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\right]+\left(\lambda\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)-\lambda^{2}\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)\right)\rho(\varepsilon).

Remark 2.26
.

In
[
46
]
,
\rho(\varepsilon)
with some
\Phi
is used to construct the polymer measure
\nu_{\lambda}
.
The author focused on a lower bound for

\displaystyle\rho(\varepsilon_{1})-\rho(\varepsilon_{2}).

for
0<\varepsilon_{1}<\varepsilon_{2}
. He avoided the discussion of differentiability of
\rho
and used the lower bound derived from the derivative of the modified function in which
{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon}
is replaced by
J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon,a}
.

Proof.

We may assume that
\Phi
is a positive random variable.
Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Let
\widetilde{\varepsilon}\in(0,1-\varepsilon)
be small enough. Then, we have

\displaystyle\rho(\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon})-\rho(\varepsilon)=-E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon},\lambda}_{0,1}\right)\left(\exp\left(\overline{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon},\lambda}_{0,1}-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}\right)-1\right)\Phi\right].

We can cope with the deterministic part in
\overline{J}^{\cdot,\lambda}_{0,1}
easily. Thus, it is enough to focus on

\displaystyle E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon},\lambda}_{0,1}\right)\left(\exp\left(\lambda\left({J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0,1}-{J}^{\varepsilon}_{0,1}\right)\right)-1\right)\Phi\right].

Since
\lambda\left({J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0,1}-{J}^{\varepsilon}_{0,1}\right)
is negative, we have

\displaystyle E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon},\lambda}_{0,1}\right)\left(\exp\left(\lambda\left({J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0,1}-{J}^{\varepsilon}_{0,1}\right)\right)-1\right)\Phi\right]

\displaystyle\geq\lambda E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon},\lambda}_{0,1}\right)\left({J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0,1}-{J}^{\varepsilon}_{0,1}\right)\Phi\right]

\displaystyle=-\lambda\int_{T_{\varepsilon}\backslash T_{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}}\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon},\lambda}_{0,1}\right)\delta_{0}(\omega_{u}-\omega_{v})\Phi\right].

It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\lim_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}\to 0}\frac{1}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\int_{T_{\varepsilon}\backslash T_{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}}\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon},\lambda}_{0,1}\right)\delta_{0}(\omega_{u}-\omega_{v})\Phi\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}sE\left[e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\Phi\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\right].

On the other hand, we can use the following bound obtained by Rosen
[
106
, p.336, just below (4.4)]
: For any
k\geq 2
and
p>4
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle E\left[\left|{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0,1}-{J}^{\varepsilon}_{0,1}\right|^{k}\right]\leq C|T_{\varepsilon}\backslash T_{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}|^{\frac{k}{p}}\leq C\widetilde{\varepsilon}^{\frac{k}{p}},

(2.27)

where
|T_{\varepsilon}\backslash T_{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}|
is the Lebesgue measure of
T_{\varepsilon}\backslash T_{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.
Therefore, for any
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\varlimsup_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}\to 0}\frac{1}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon},\lambda}_{0,1}\right)\left(\exp\left({J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0,1}-{J}^{\varepsilon}_{0,1}\right)-1\right)\Phi:{J}^{\varepsilon}_{0,1}-{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0,1}>\delta\right]=0.

Let
r>0
be a small constant and take
\delta>0
such that
1-e^{-x}\geq(1-r)x
for
0\leq x\leq\delta
. Then,

\displaystyle E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon},\lambda}_{0,1}\right)\left(\exp\left(\lambda\left({J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0,1}-{J}^{\varepsilon}_{0,1}\right)\right)-1\right)\Phi\right]

\displaystyle\leq-(1-r)\lambda E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon},\lambda}_{0,1}\right)\left({J}^{\varepsilon}_{0,1}-{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0,1}\right)\Phi:{J}^{\varepsilon}_{0,1}-{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0,1}\leq\delta\right]+o(\widetilde{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\leq-(1-r)\lambda E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon},\lambda}_{0,1}\right)\left({J}^{\varepsilon}_{0,1}-{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0,1}\right)\Phi\right]+o(\widetilde{\varepsilon}),

where we have used (
2.27
) in the last line.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle\varlimsup_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}\to 0}\frac{1}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon},\lambda}_{0,1}\right)\left(\exp\left(\lambda\left({J}^{\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0,1}-{J}^{\varepsilon}_{0,1}\right)\right)-1\right)\Phi\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1-r)\lambda\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}sE\left[e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\Phi\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\right].

Since
0<r<1
is arbitrary, the right differentiability follows. The left differentiability follows by the same kind of argument.

3
Some estimates with respect to
\nu_{\varepsilon,\lambda}

3.1
Recursive estimates

For
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, we set
p_{t}(x):=(2\pi t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{|x|^{2}}{2t}\right)
, and

\displaystyle g_{t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x):=E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right)\delta_{x}(\omega_{t})\right].

In
[
46
]
and
[
27
]
it was shown that for any given
\lambda\in[0,\infty)
there is a constant
C\in(0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\left|g_{t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)-p_{t}(x)\right|\leq Ct^{\frac{1}{2}}p_{2t}(x)

(3.1)

for all
t\in[0,1]
and
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
.
Here, we remark that the constant
C
in (
3.1
) can be chosen independently of
\lambda
, at least for small
\lambda
.
For
n\geq 1
, we define an
n
-points “transition density” by

\displaystyle g_{\mathbbm{t}}^{T,\varepsilon,\lambda}(\mathbf{x}):=E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,T}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right)\delta_{x_{1}}(\omega_{t_{1}})\cdots\delta_{x_{n}}(\omega_{t_{n}})\right],

for
\mathbbm{t}=(t_{1},\cdots,t_{n})
with
0<t_{1}<\cdots<t_{n}\leq T\leq 1
and
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\in({\mathbb{R}}^{3})^{n}
.
The following lemma is provided in
[
27
, Lemma 4.1]
.

Lemma 3.1
.

For any
n\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C\in(0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle g_{\mathbbm{t}}^{T,\varepsilon,\lambda}(\mathbf{x})\leq Cp_{2t_{1}}(x_{1})p_{2(t_{2}-t_{1})}(x_{2}-x_{1})\cdots p_{2(t_{n}-t_{n-1})}(x_{n}-x_{n-1}).

for
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\in({\mathbb{R}}^{3})^{n}
,
\lambda\in[0,\infty)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
.

Let
\psi:[0,1]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be bounded and infinitely often differentiable with bounded derivatives of all orders, and for
0\leq s<t\leq 1
, we define
\Psi_{s,t}
by

\displaystyle\Psi_{s,t}:=\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\psi(u,\omega_{u})\text{\rm d}u\right).

(3.2)

As was shown in
[
46
, §4]
, (
3.1
) implies that for any such
\psi
and
\lambda\in[0,\infty)
there is a constant
C\in(0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{1},\lambda}\right)\Psi_{0,1}\right]-E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{2},\lambda}\right)\Psi_{0,1}\right]\right|\leq C(\varepsilon_{1}^{\delta}+\varepsilon_{2}^{\delta})

(3.3)

for all
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
, where
\delta\in(0,1)
. This implies that the limit

\displaystyle N(\lambda)=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right)\right]\in(0,\infty)

(3.4)

exists for all
\lambda\in[0,\infty)
. Strict positivity was proved in
[
27
]
.
In this section, we will give a similar estimate for more general functions
\Phi
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a bounded measurable function. Let
\Delta_{u,v;s,t}:=\{(\sigma,\tau):u\leq\sigma\leq v,s\leq\tau\leq t\}
, and for any fixed
a\in(0,1)
set

\displaystyle\Phi_{u,v;s,t}:=\int_{T_{a}\cap\Delta_{u,v;s,t}}f(\omega_{\sigma}-\omega_{\tau})\text{\rm d}\sigma\text{\rm d}\tau

(3.5)

where
T_{a}:=\{(s,t)\in[0,1]^{2}:t-s\geq a\}
as above.
For short, we set
\Phi:=\Phi_{0,1;0,1}
and
\Phi_{s,t}:=\Phi_{s,t;s,t}
. We set

\displaystyle\rho(\varepsilon):=E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right)\Phi\right].

(3.6)

The main aim of this section is to prove the following lemma, that will be used in the proof of Theorem
4.1
below.

Lemma 3.2
.

There are constants
C\in(0,\infty)
and
\widetilde{\delta}\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle|\rho(\varepsilon_{1})-\rho({\varepsilon_{2}})|\leq C\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}|f(x)|(\varepsilon_{1}^{\widetilde{\delta}}+\varepsilon_{2}^{\widetilde{\delta}})

(3.7)

for
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
.

The proof of Lemma
3.2
will be provided in Section
3.2
below.
The estimate (
3.7
), with
|\Phi|
replaced by
\Psi_{0,1}
in (
3.2
), was already proved by Bolthausen in
[
46
, §4]
. By modifying his argument one can see that the discussion in
[
46
, §4]
is also suitable for the present case. Since
|\Phi_{0,{t+s}}|
is not equal to
|\Phi_{0,s}||\Phi_{s,s+t}|
, some arguments given in the proof of Lemma
3.2
below are different from those given in
[
46
, §4]
.

Remark 3.3
.

•

Lemma
3.2
is true for
|\Phi|
where
\Phi=\Phi_{1}-\Phi_{2}
for
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}
of the form of the
\Phi
entering in (
3.5
).
A typical example of
\Phi
to which Lemma
3.2
applies is:

\displaystyle\Phi:=\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u,h)-\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n-1},a_{n-1}}(u,h)\quad h\in K

where
\widetilde{J}
was defined just after Corollary
2.2
.

•

We are able to generalize (
3.7
) as follows:
Let
h
be a given function in
K_{0}
(with
K_{0}
is as in Theorem
1.7
and
K
as in Section
1.3
) and
U_{s,t}(h):=h^{\prime}_{t}\omega_{t}-h^{\prime}_{s}\omega_{s}-\int_{s}^{t}\omega_{\tau}h^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\text{\rm d}\tau
with
U_{1}(h):=U_{0,1}(h)
.
Let
F
be an element of
C_{b}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{n+2})
, the set of continuously differentiable functions
F
with
\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+2}}|F(x)|+\sum_{|\alpha|=1}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+2}}|\partial_{\alpha}F(x)|<\infty
.
Then, we have (with
\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}
is given after (
1.7
))

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{1},\lambda}\right)F(\Phi,U_{1},\omega_{t_{1}},\cdots,\omega_{t_{n}})\right]-E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{2},\lambda}\right)F(\Phi,U_{1},\omega_{t_{1}},\cdots,\omega_{t_{n}})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+2}}|F(x)|+\max|f|\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+2}}|\partial_{x_{1}}F(x)|+\sum_{i=2}^{n+2}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+2}}|\partial_{x_{i}}F(x)|\right)(\varepsilon_{1}^{\delta_{0}}+\varepsilon_{2}^{\delta_{0}}),

for any
0\leq t_{1}<\cdots<t_{n}\leq 1
.

•

We also have another generalization:
For
i=1,\cdots,m
, let
f_{i}:{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be any bounded measurable function. For
a_{i}\in(0,1)
and
i=1,\cdots,m
, set

\displaystyle\Psi^{(i)}_{u,v;s,t}:=\int_{T_{a_{i}}\cap\Delta_{u,v;s,t}}f_{i}(\omega_{\tau}-\omega_{\sigma})\text{\rm d}\sigma\text{\rm d}\tau.

Then, we have that for any given
\ell\geq 1
, there are constants
C\in(0,\infty)
and
\widetilde{\delta}\in(0,1)
such that (with
\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}
is given after (
1.7
))

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{1},\lambda}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{m}\Psi^{(i)}_{0,1;0,1}\right]-E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{2},\lambda}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{m}\Psi^{(i)}_{0,1;0,1}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\prod_{i=1}^{m}\max_{x\in R^{3}}|f_{i}(x)|\left(\varepsilon_{1}^{\widetilde{\delta}}+\varepsilon_{2}^{\widetilde{\delta}}\right)

for
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
.
For the proof, we remark that the same argument as the proof of Lemma
3.2
can be applied in view of the fact that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\prod_{i=1}^{n}(x_{i}+v_{i})-\prod_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}\right|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}|v_{i}|\prod_{j\not=i}|x_{j}+v_{j}|\vee|x_{j}|

for any
x_{i},v_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
(
i=1,2,\dots,n
).

Remark 3.4
.

Lemma
3.2
is the same as the one in
[
23
]
and the idea of the proof is based on the one in
[
23
]
(where the detailed estimation of some integrals was omitted, but we confirm that it can be provided).

3.2
Proof of Lemma
3.2

Proof of Lemma
3.2
.

For short, we will denote
\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f(x)|
by
\max|f|
. Also, we remark that
\rho(\varepsilon)\leq C\max|f|
for
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2}
.
From Lemma
2.25
, we have the following relation:

\displaystyle\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\rho(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\lambda\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}sE\left[e^{-\overline{J}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}_{0,1}}\Phi\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\right]+\left(\lambda\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)-\lambda^{2}\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)\right)\rho(\varepsilon).

(3.8)

We remark that in
[
46
]
the differentiability of
\rho(\varepsilon)
is not provided.

We decompose
\overline{J}
as follows (see Figure
1
):

\displaystyle\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}

\displaystyle=\lambda J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon}-\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)+\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\lambda J_{0,s}^{\varepsilon}-\lambda s\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)+\lambda^{2}s\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda J_{s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon}-\lambda(1-s-\varepsilon)\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)+\lambda^{2}(1-s-\varepsilon)\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda J_{0,s;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon}+\lambda J_{0,s;s,s+\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}+\lambda J_{s,s+\varepsilon;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon(\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)-\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon))

\displaystyle=\overline{J}_{0,s}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}+\overline{J}_{s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}+\lambda J_{0,s;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon}+\lambda J_{0,s;s,s+\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}+\lambda J_{s,s+\varepsilon;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon(\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)-\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon))

(3.9)

and set

\displaystyle\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}:=\overline{J}_{0,s}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}+\overline{J}_{s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}+\lambda J_{0,s;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon}.

Remark 3.5
.

The readers have to be careful not to confuse it with the similar notation
\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon,a}(u,h)
given just after Corollary
2.2
.

Figure 1:
A decomposition of
J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon}
.

It is obvious from the definition of
\kappa_{1}
in (
1.4
) and
\kappa_{2}
entering (
1.7
) that

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon,\lambda}:=\varepsilon(\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)-\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon))=O(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}).

(3.10)

Using the fact that
1-x\leq e^{-x}\leq 1-x+\frac{1}{2}x^{2}
for
x>0
, one shows that

\displaystyle E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\Phi\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\Phi\right]e^{K_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})(J_{0,s;s,s+\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}+J_{s,s+\varepsilon;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon})\Phi\right]e^{K_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}

\displaystyle\quad+R_{\lambda,s,\varepsilon},

(3.11)

where the remainder term
R_{\lambda,s,\varepsilon}
is dominated by

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})(J_{0,s;s,s+\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}+J_{s,s+\varepsilon;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon})^{2}|\Phi|\right]e^{K_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}.

Substituting this into (
3.8
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\rho(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\lambda\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}sE\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\Phi\right]e^{K_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}

\displaystyle-\lambda^{2}\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}sE\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})(J_{0,s;s,s+\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}+J_{s,s+\varepsilon;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon})\Phi\right]e^{K_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}sR_{\lambda,s,\varepsilon}+\left(\lambda\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)-\lambda^{2}\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)\right)\rho(\varepsilon).

(3.12)

We set

\displaystyle I_{\varepsilon,s}^{(1)}:=E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\Phi\right],

\displaystyle I_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2)}:=E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})(J_{0,s;s,s+\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}+J_{s,s+\varepsilon;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon})\Phi\right],

\displaystyle I_{\varepsilon,s}^{(3)}:=E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})(J_{0,s;s,s+\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}+J_{s,s+\varepsilon;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\Phi\right].

and will prove that there exists
\widetilde{\delta}\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}I_{\varepsilon,s}^{(1)}\text{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=-(1-\varepsilon)\rho(\varepsilon)\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)+\lambda(1-\varepsilon)\varepsilon\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)\rho(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\quad+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\widetilde{\delta}-1}

(3.13)

\displaystyle=-(1-\varepsilon)(1-\varepsilon\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon))\rho(\varepsilon)\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\widetilde{\delta}-1}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}I_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2)}\text{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=-\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)\rho(\varepsilon)+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\widetilde{\delta}-1}

(3.14)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}I_{\varepsilon,s}^{(3)}\text{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(3.15)

(
3.13
)-(
3.15
) will be proven in Sections
3.2.1
–
3.2.3
below.
Once we obtain (
3.13
)-(
3.15
), we deduce from (
3.12
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\rho(\varepsilon)=

\displaystyle-\lambda\left((1-\varepsilon)(1-\varepsilon\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon))e^{K_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}-1\right)\rho(\varepsilon)\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+\lambda^{2}\left(e^{K_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}-1\right)\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)\rho(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\widetilde{\delta}-1}e^{K_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{K_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}.

On the other hand, we find from (
1.5
), (
1.7
), and (
3.10
) that

\displaystyle(1-\varepsilon)(1-\varepsilon\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon))e^{K_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}-1

\displaystyle=(1-\varepsilon)(1-\varepsilon\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon))\left(1+\varepsilon\left(\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)-\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)\right)+O(\varepsilon)\right)-1

\displaystyle=O\left(\varepsilon\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle e^{K_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}-1

\displaystyle=\left(1+O\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right)-1=O\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\right),

and hence we have

\displaystyle\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\rho(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=O\left(\varepsilon\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)\rho(\varepsilon)\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)+\lambda^{2}O(1)\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho(\varepsilon)\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{-\max\{\frac{1}{2},1-\widetilde{\delta}\}}

\displaystyle=O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\widehat{\delta}-1},

for some
\widehat{\delta}\in\left(0,\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\widetilde{\delta}\right\}\right)
, where we have used (
A.8
).
From this, we complete the proof of Lemma
3.1
.

3.2.1
Proof of (
3.15
)

Proof.

The Cauchy-Schwarz inequality implies that

\displaystyle\left|I_{\varepsilon,s}^{(3)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})(J_{0,s;s,s+\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}+J_{s,s+\varepsilon;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon})^{2}|\Phi|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})(|J_{0,s;s,s+\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}+|J_{s,s+\varepsilon;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon}|^{2})|\Phi|\right].

By definition, we know that

\displaystyle|J_{0,s;s,s+\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}=\int_{([0,s]\times[s,s+\varepsilon])^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}1\{\tau_{i}-\sigma_{i}\geq\varepsilon\}\delta_{0}(\omega_{\sigma_{i}}-\omega_{\tau_{i}})\text{\rm d}\tau_{i}\text{\rm d}\sigma_{i}

and by using Lemma
3.1
, we obtain that

\displaystyle E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\delta_{0}(\omega_{\sigma_{1}}-\omega_{\tau_{1}})\delta_{0}(\omega_{\sigma_{2}}-\omega_{\tau_{2}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\displaystyle C\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\text{\rm d}x\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\text{\rm d}y\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\text{\rm d}zp_{2\sigma_{1}}(x)p_{2(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})}(x,y)p_{2(s-\sigma_{2})}(y,z)\\
\hskip 70.0001pt\times p_{2(\tau_{1}-s)}(z,x)p_{2(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})}(x,y)p_{2(s+\varepsilon-\tau_{2})}(y,z)\\
\hskip 100.00015pt\text{if }0<\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<s<\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}<s+\varepsilon;\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle C\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\text{\rm d}x\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\text{\rm d}y\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\text{\rm d}zp_{2\sigma_{1}}(x)p_{2(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})}(x,y)p_{2(s-\sigma_{2})}(y,z)\\
\hskip 70.0001pt\times p_{2(\tau_{2}-s)}(z,y)p_{2(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})}(y,x)p_{2(s+\varepsilon-\tau_{1})}(x,z)\\
\hskip 100.00015pt\text{if }0<\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<s<\tau_{2}<\tau_{1}<s+\varepsilon.\end{cases}

(3.16)

By the relations

\displaystyle p_{s}(x)p_{t}(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi(s+t))^{3/2}}p_{\frac{st}{s+t}}(x),

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}p_{s}(x,y)p_{t}(y,z)\text{\rm d}y=p_{s+t}(x,z),

the right-hand side in (
3.16
) is given by

\displaystyle C({(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})}({s+\varepsilon-\tau_{2}+s-\sigma_{2}})+{({\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}+\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}})(s+\varepsilon-\tau_{2})(s-\sigma_{2})}

\displaystyle+({\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}+\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}})({s+\varepsilon-\tau_{2}+s-\sigma_{2}})(\tau_{1}-s))^{-\frac{3}{2}}

for
0<\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<s<\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}<s+\varepsilon
and

\displaystyle C{(\tau_{2}-\sigma_{2})(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})}+{(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}+\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})(s-\sigma_{2})(\tau_{2}-s)}

\displaystyle+(\tau_{2}-\sigma_{2})(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}+\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})(s+\varepsilon-\tau_{1}))^{-\frac{3}{2}}

for
0<\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<s<\tau_{2}<\tau_{1}<s+\varepsilon
.

Figure 2:
The transitions for the case that
0<\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<s<\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}<s+\varepsilon
. When we regard it as an electrical circuit, the computation technique of the resistance helps us to compute the integrals with respect to the space variables
x,y,z
.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})|J_{0,s;s,s+\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}|\Phi|\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|\int_{0<\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<s<\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}<s+\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}\sigma_{1}\text{\rm d}\tau_{1}\text{\rm d}\sigma_{2}\text{\rm d}\tau_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 113.81102pt[{(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})}({s+\varepsilon-\tau_{2}+s-\sigma_{2}})

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 113.81102pt+{({\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}+\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}})(s+\varepsilon-\tau_{2})(s-\sigma_{2})}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 113.81102pt+({\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}+\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}})({s+\varepsilon-\tau_{2}+s-\sigma_{2}})(\tau_{1}-s)]^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\max|f|\int_{0<\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<s<\tau_{2}<\tau_{1}<s+\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}\sigma_{1}\text{\rm d}\tau_{1}\text{\rm d}\sigma_{2}\text{\rm d}\tau_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 113.81102pt[(\tau_{2}-\sigma_{2})(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 113.81102pt+(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}+\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})(s-\sigma_{2})(\tau_{2}-s)

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 113.81102pt+(\tau_{2}-\sigma_{2})(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}+\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})(s+\varepsilon-\tau_{1})]^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

Changing variables
\tau_{i}-s=\varepsilon t_{i}
,
s-\sigma_{i}=\varepsilon s_{i}
(
i=1,2
), we have

\displaystyle E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})|J_{0,s;s,s+\varepsilon}|^{2}|\Phi|\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|{\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\iint_{0<s_{2}<s_{1}<\infty,0<t_{1}<t_{2}<1}\text{\rm d}s_{1}\text{\rm d}s_{2}\text{\rm d}t_{1}\text{\rm d}t_{2}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 113.81102pt[(s_{1}-s_{2})(t_{2}-t_{1})(1-t_{2}+s_{2})+(s_{1}-s_{2}+t_{2}-t_{1})(1-t_{2})s_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 227.62204pt+(s_{1}-s_{2}+t_{2}-t_{1})(1-t_{2}+s_{2})t_{1}]^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\iint_{0<s_{2}<s_{1}<\infty,0<t_{2}<t_{1}<1}\text{\rm d}s_{1}\text{\rm d}s_{2}\text{\rm d}t_{1}\text{\rm d}t_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 113.81102pt[(t_{2}+s_{2})(s_{1}-s_{2})(t_{1}-t_{2})+(s_{1}-s_{2}+t_{1}-t_{2})s_{2}t_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 199.16928pt\left.\phantom{\int}+(t_{2}+s_{2})(s_{1}-s_{2}+t_{1}-t_{2})(1-t_{1})]^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=C{\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}}.

(3.17)

For details see Appendix
A.1
.

Similarly, we have that

\displaystyle E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})|J_{s,s+\varepsilon;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}|\Phi|\right]\leq C{\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}}.

In this way we then obtain (
3.15
).

3.2.2
Proof of (
3.13
)

Before starting the proof of (
3.13
), we give two propositions.

Proposition 3.6
.

We take
(s,t)
and
u,v
such as

\displaystyle 0\leq s\leq 1-\varepsilon,\quad

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon\leq t\leq 1

\displaystyle 0\leq u\leq s\leq v\leq t,\quad

\displaystyle v-u\leq\varepsilon.

Then we have

\displaystyle E\left[\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})e^{-\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{v,t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda J_{0,u;v,t}^{\varepsilon}}\Phi_{0,t}\right]

\displaystyle=p_{v-u}(0)E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,t-(v-u)}^{{\varepsilon,\lambda}}-\lambda Y_{u,v}}\Phi_{0,t-(v-u)}\right]+O(1)\max|f|(v-u)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(3.18)

where

\displaystyle Y_{u,v}:=Y_{u,v}^{t,\varepsilon}=\iint_{\left\{\begin{smallmatrix}(u-\varepsilon)\vee 0<\sigma<u\\
u<\tau<(u+\varepsilon)\wedge(t-(v-u))\\
u-(v-\varepsilon)<\tau-\sigma<\varepsilon\end{smallmatrix}\right\}}\text{\rm d}\sigma\text{\rm d}\tau\delta_{0}(\omega_{\tau}-\omega_{\sigma}).

Figure 3:
The domains in the integrals
\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}
,
\overline{J}_{v,t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}
, and
J_{0,u;v,t}^{\varepsilon}
.

Figure 4:
The domains in the integral is shifted by the Markov property of Brownian motion.

Proof.

By definition we know that

\displaystyle|\Phi_{0,u;u,v}|+|\Phi_{u,v;v,t}|+\left|\Phi_{u,v;u,v}\right|\leq C\max|f|(v-u).

This and Lemma
3.1
imply that

\displaystyle E\left[\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})e^{-\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{v,t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda J_{0,u;v,t}^{\varepsilon}}\left(|\Phi_{0,u;u,v}|+|\Phi_{u,v;v,t}|+\left|\Phi_{u,v;u,v}\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|(v-u)E\left[\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})e^{-\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{v,t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|(v-u)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}yg_{u}(x)p_{v-u}(0)g_{t-v}(y-x)

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|(v-u)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Similarly we have

\displaystyle E\left[\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})e^{-\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{v,t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda J_{0,u;v,t}^{\varepsilon}}\left|\int_{T_{a}\cap\left\{\begin{smallmatrix}(u-a)\vee 0<\sigma<u\\
v<\tau<(v+a)\wedge t\\
\tau-\sigma\leq v-u+a\end{smallmatrix}\right\}}f(\omega_{\tau}-\omega_{\sigma})\text{\rm d}\tau\text{\rm d}\sigma\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|(v-u)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

We denote
\Theta_{a,u,v}:=\int_{T_{a}\cap\left\{\begin{smallmatrix}(u-a)\vee 0<\sigma<u\\
v<\tau<(v+a)\wedge t\\
\tau-\sigma\leq v-u+a\end{smallmatrix}\right\}}f(\omega_{\tau}-\omega_{\sigma})\text{\rm d}\tau\text{\rm d}\sigma
. (See Figure
5
below.)

Figure 5:
Decomposition of the integral of
\Phi
.

This shows that

\displaystyle E\left[\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})e^{-\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{v,t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda J_{0,u;v,t}^{\varepsilon}}\Phi_{0,t}\right]

\displaystyle=O(1)\max|f|(v-u)^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{v,t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda J_{0,u;v,t}^{\varepsilon}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})\left(\Phi_{0,t}-\Phi_{0,u;u,v}-\Phi_{u,v;v,t}-\Phi_{u,v;u,v}-\Theta_{a,u,v}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=O(1)\max|f|(v-u)^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{v,t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda J_{0,u;v,t}^{\varepsilon}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})\left(\Phi_{0,u}+\Phi_{v,t}+\Phi_{0,u;v,t}-\Theta_{a,u,v}\right)\right].

By the Markov property of Brownian motion and the shift invariance of
J
we have that conditioned on
\omega_{v}=\omega_{u}
, the following equality in distribution holds.

\displaystyle(\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}+\overline{J}_{v,t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}+\lambda J_{0,u;v,t}^{\varepsilon},\Phi_{0,u},\Phi_{v,t},\Phi_{0,u;v,t}-\Theta_{a,u,v})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(\overline{J}_{0,t-(v-u)}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}+\lambda Y_{u,v},\Phi_{0,u},\Phi_{u,t-(v-u)},\Phi_{0,u;u,t-(v-u)}).

From this we have

\displaystyle E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{v,t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda J_{0,u;v,t}^{\varepsilon}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})\left(\Phi_{0,u}+\Phi_{v,t}+\Phi_{0,u;v,t}-\Theta_{a,u,v}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=p_{v-u}(0)E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,t-(v-u)}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda Y_{u,v}}(\Phi_{0,u}+\Phi_{u,t-(v-u)}+\Phi_{0,u;u,t-(v-u)})\right]

\displaystyle=p_{v-u}(0)E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,t-(v-u)}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda Y_{u,v}}\Phi_{0,t-(v-u)}\right].

From this equality and the inequalities above we obtain the desired estimate of this proposition.

Lemma 3.7

(cf.
[
46
, (4.6)]
)
.

There exists
\widetilde{\delta}>0
such that for sufficiently small
\varepsilon>0
and
r\leq 2\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle\left|E\left[e^{-\bar{J}_{0,1-r}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\Phi\right]-E\left[e^{-\bar{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\Phi\right]\right|\leq C\max|f|\varepsilon^{\widetilde{\delta}-\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

This estimate is proved similarly to
[
46
, (4.6)]
.
We omit the detailed proof.

Proof of (
3.13
).

Applying Proposition
3.6
for
u=s
,
v=s+\varepsilon
, and
t=1
, we have

\displaystyle I_{\varepsilon,s}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}+p_{\varepsilon}(0)E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda Y_{s,s+\varepsilon}}\Phi_{0,1-\varepsilon}\right]

\displaystyle=O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}+p_{\varepsilon}(0)\left(E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\Phi_{0,1-\varepsilon}\right]-\lambda E\left[Y_{s,s+\varepsilon}e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\Phi_{0,1-\varepsilon}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt\left.+O(1)\lambda^{2}E\left[Y_{s,s+\varepsilon}^{2}e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\Phi_{0,1-\varepsilon}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=:O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}+p_{\varepsilon}(0)\left(A_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}-\lambda A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2)}+O(1)\lambda^{2}A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(3)}\right).

Here, we note that
p_{\varepsilon}(0)=-\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)
.
Since

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}=E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\Phi_{0,1}\right]+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon,

Lemma
3.7
gives that

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}=\rho(\varepsilon)+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(3)}=E\left[Y_{s,s+\varepsilon}^{2}e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\Phi_{0,1-\varepsilon}\right]\leq C\max|f|\varepsilon

(3.19)

(see Appendix
A.2
) and hence,

\displaystyle p_{\varepsilon}(0)\lambda^{2}A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(3)}\leq C\max|f|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}

for
0\leq s\leq 1-\varepsilon
.

In the rest, we will prove that

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2)}=\begin{cases}O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},&1-2\varepsilon\leq s\leq 1-\varepsilon\\
\varepsilon\left(\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)\rho(\varepsilon)+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon,\quad&\varepsilon\leq s\leq 1-2\varepsilon\\
\frac{2}{(\sqrt{2\pi})^{3}}\left(2\sqrt{s}-\frac{s}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\rho(\varepsilon)+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon,\quad&0\leq s\leq\varepsilon\end{cases}

which implies that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2)}\text{\rm d}s=(1-\varepsilon)\varepsilon\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)\rho(\varepsilon)+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon,

which then, completes the proof of (
3.13
).

Since

\displaystyle E\left[Y_{s,s+\varepsilon}e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}|\Phi_{0,1-\varepsilon}-\Phi_{0,1}|\right]\leq C\max|f|\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}},

we have

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2)}=E\left[Y_{s,s+\varepsilon}e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\Phi_{0,1-\varepsilon}\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[Y_{s,s+\varepsilon}e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\Phi_{0,1}\right]+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=E\left[\int\int_{0\leq u\leq s\leq v\leq 1-\varepsilon,v-u\leq\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\Phi_{0,1}\right]+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=:A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1)}+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}.

As in (
3.11
), we have

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1)}

\displaystyle=E\left[\int\int_{0\leq u\leq s\leq v\leq 1-\varepsilon,v-u\leq\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\Phi_{0,1}\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[\int\int_{0\leq u\leq s\leq v\leq 1-\varepsilon,v-u\leq\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})e^{-\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{v,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda J_{0,u;v,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}}\Phi_{0,1}e^{K_{\varepsilon,v-u,\lambda}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-O(1)\lambda E\left[\int\int_{0\leq u\leq s\leq v\leq 1-\varepsilon,v-u\leq\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})e^{-\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{v,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda J_{0,u;v,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}}\Phi_{0,1}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 250.00038pt\times(J_{0,u;u,v}^{\varepsilon}+J_{u,v;v,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon})e^{K_{\varepsilon,v-u,\lambda}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=:A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1,1)}-O(1)\lambda A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1,2)},

where

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon,\theta,\lambda}:=\theta(\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)-\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)).

Using the same argument as in the proof of (
3.15
), one proves that

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1,2)}=O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon

(3.20)

for
0\leq s\leq 1-\varepsilon
(see Appendix
A.3
).

Applying Proposition
3.6
, we get that

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1,1)}

\displaystyle=E\left[\iint_{0\leq u\leq s\leq v\leq 1-\varepsilon,v-u\leq\varepsilon}\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})e^{-\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{v,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda J_{0,u;v,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}}\Phi_{0,1}e^{K_{\varepsilon,v-u,\lambda}}\right]

\displaystyle=\iint_{0\leq u\leq s\leq v\leq 1-\varepsilon,v-u\leq\varepsilon}p_{v-u}(0)E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon-(v-u)}^{{\varepsilon,\lambda}}-\lambda Y_{u,v}}\Phi_{0,1-\varepsilon-(v-u)}\right]e^{K_{\varepsilon,v-u,\lambda}}\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=\iint_{0\leq u\leq s\leq v\leq 1-\varepsilon,v-u\leq\varepsilon}p_{v-u}(0)E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon-(v-u)}^{{\varepsilon,\lambda}}}\Phi_{0,1-\varepsilon-(v-u)}\right]e^{K_{\varepsilon,v-u,\lambda}}\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v

\displaystyle-O(1)\lambda\iint_{0\leq u\leq s\leq v\leq 1-\varepsilon,v-u\leq\varepsilon}p_{v-u}(0)E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon-(v-u)}^{{\varepsilon,\lambda}}}\Phi_{0,1-\varepsilon-(v-u)}Y_{u,v}\right]e^{K_{\varepsilon,v-u,\lambda}}\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=:A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1,1,1)}-O(1)\lambda A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1,1,2)}+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}.

By Lemma
3.1
, we know that

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1,1,2)}

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{0\leq u\leq s\leq v\leq 1-\varepsilon,v-u\leq\varepsilon}p_{v-u}(0)E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon-(v-u)}^{{\varepsilon,\lambda}}}\Phi_{0,1-\varepsilon-(v-u)}Y_{u,v}\right]\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v

\displaystyle=O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon

for
0\leq s\leq 1-\varepsilon
.
Since

\displaystyle\iint_{0\leq u\leq s\leq v\leq 1-\varepsilon,v-u\leq\varepsilon}p_{v-u}(0)\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v=\begin{cases}4\sqrt{1-\varepsilon-s}-2\frac{1-\varepsilon-s}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}},\quad&1-2\varepsilon\leq s\leq 1-\varepsilon\\
\varepsilon\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\varepsilon,\quad&\varepsilon\leq s\leq 1-2\varepsilon\\
\frac{2}{(\sqrt{2\pi})^{3}}\left(2\sqrt{s}-\frac{s}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right),\quad&0\leq s\leq\varepsilon\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon-(v-u)}^{{\varepsilon,\lambda}}}\left|\Phi_{0,1-\varepsilon-(v-u)}-\Phi_{0,1}\right|\right]=O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon,\quad\text{for }0<v-u<\varepsilon,

Lemma
3.7
yields that

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1,1,1)}=\begin{cases}O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}&1-2\varepsilon\leq s\leq 1-\varepsilon\\
\varepsilon\left(\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)\rho(\varepsilon)+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon,\quad&\varepsilon\leq s\leq 1-2\varepsilon\\
\frac{2}{(\sqrt{2\pi})^{3}}\left(2\sqrt{s}-\frac{s}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\rho(\varepsilon)+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon\quad&0\leq s\leq\varepsilon\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1,1)}=\begin{cases}O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},&1-2\varepsilon\leq s\leq 1-\varepsilon\\
\varepsilon\left(\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)\rho(\varepsilon)+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon,\quad&\varepsilon\leq s\leq 1-2\varepsilon\\
\frac{2}{(\sqrt{2\pi})^{3}}\left(2\sqrt{s}-\frac{s}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\rho(\varepsilon)+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon\quad&0\leq s\leq\varepsilon.\end{cases}

Putting things together, we obtain that

\displaystyle A^{(2)}_{\varepsilon,s}=\begin{cases}O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},&1-2\varepsilon\leq s\leq 1-\varepsilon\\
\varepsilon\left(\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)\rho(\varepsilon)+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon,\quad&\varepsilon\leq s\leq 1-2\varepsilon\\
\frac{2}{(\sqrt{2\pi})^{3}}\left(2\sqrt{s}-\frac{s}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\rho(\varepsilon)+O(1)\max|f|\varepsilon\quad&0\leq s\leq\varepsilon.\end{cases}

3.2.3
Proof of (
3.14
)

Let

\displaystyle I_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1)}:=E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})J_{0,s;s,s+\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}\Phi\right],

\displaystyle I_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,2)}:=E\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})J_{0,s+\varepsilon;s+\varepsilon,1}^{\varepsilon}\Phi\right].

As in
[
46
, §4]
, we choose
1>\beta>\gamma>\frac{1}{2}>\alpha>0
.
Letting

\displaystyle T^{1}(\varepsilon^{\alpha})

\displaystyle:=\{(s,u,v):s<\varepsilon^{\alpha},0<u<s<v<s+\varepsilon,v-u\geq\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\qquad\cup\{(s,u,v):s>1-\varepsilon^{\alpha},0<u<s<v<s+\varepsilon,v-u\geq\varepsilon\},

\displaystyle T^{2}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})

\displaystyle:=\{(s,u,v):\varepsilon^{\alpha}<s<1-\varepsilon^{\alpha},0<u<s-\varepsilon^{\beta}<s<v<s+\varepsilon,v-u\geq\varepsilon\},

we find that

\displaystyle\int_{T^{1}(\varepsilon^{\alpha})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\delta_{0}(\omega_{u}-\omega_{v})|\Phi|\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|\int_{T^{1}(\varepsilon^{\alpha})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\int\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}yp_{2u}(x)p_{2(s-u)}(x,y)p_{2(v-s)}(y,x)p_{2(s+\varepsilon-v)}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|\varepsilon^{\alpha-1}

and

\displaystyle\int_{T^{2}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\delta_{0}(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})|\Phi|\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|\int_{T^{2}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\int\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}yp_{2u}(x)p_{2(s-u)}(x,y)p_{2(v-s)}(y,x)p_{2(s+\varepsilon-v)}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|\varepsilon^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2}}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}sI_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1)}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\int_{u<s<v<s+\varepsilon,v-u>\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\delta_{0}(\omega_{u}-\omega_{v})\Phi\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{\varepsilon^{\alpha}}^{1-\varepsilon^{\alpha}}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s-\varepsilon^{\beta}}^{s}\text{\rm d}u\int_{s\vee(u+\varepsilon)}^{s+\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\delta_{0}(\omega_{u}-\omega_{v})\Phi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\max|f|O\left(\varepsilon^{(\alpha-1)\wedge\left(-\frac{\beta+1}{2}\right)}\right).

We write

\displaystyle T^{3}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})

\displaystyle:=\{(s,u,v):\varepsilon^{\alpha}<s<1-\varepsilon^{\alpha},s-\varepsilon^{\beta}<u<s<v<s+\varepsilon,v-u\geq\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle=\{(s,u,v):\varepsilon^{\alpha}<s<1-\varepsilon^{\alpha},s-\varepsilon^{\beta}<u<s\vee(u+\varepsilon)<v<s+\varepsilon\}.

For
\varepsilon^{\gamma}\leq s\leq 1-\varepsilon^{\gamma}
, we set

\displaystyle\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}:=\overline{J}_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}+\overline{J}_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}+\lambda J_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}^{\varepsilon}.

Let us recall that
1>\beta>\gamma>\frac{1}{2}>\alpha
.
We note then that the deterministic part in

\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}
derived from
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
is of order
\varepsilon^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}
and the random part comes from the intersection local time is positive and dominated by

\displaystyle\lambda\left(J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon}-J_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}^{\varepsilon}-J_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}^{\varepsilon}-J^{\varepsilon}_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda\left(J^{\varepsilon}_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma};s-\varepsilon^{\gamma},s+\varepsilon^{\gamma}}+J^{\varepsilon}_{s-\varepsilon^{\gamma},s+\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}+J_{s-\varepsilon^{\gamma},s+\varepsilon^{\gamma}}^{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda\left(J^{\varepsilon}_{0,s+\varepsilon^{\gamma};s-\varepsilon^{\gamma},s+\varepsilon^{\gamma}}+J^{\varepsilon}_{s-\varepsilon^{\gamma},s+\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}\right)

(3.21)

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\int_{T^{3}_{s,u,v}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\widetilde{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\delta_{0}(\omega_{u}-\omega_{v})\Phi\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{T^{3}_{s,u,v}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\delta_{0}(\omega_{u}-\omega_{v})\Phi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(\varepsilon^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{T^{3}_{s,u,v}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\delta_{0}(\omega_{u}-\omega_{v})|\Phi|\right]

\displaystyle\quad+O(1)\int_{T^{3}_{s,u,v}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}J_{0,s+\varepsilon^{\gamma};s-\varepsilon^{\gamma},s+\varepsilon^{\gamma}}^{\varepsilon}\delta_{0}({\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon}})\delta_{0}({\omega_{u}-\omega_{v}})|\Phi|\right]

\displaystyle\quad+O(1)\int_{T^{3}_{s,u,v}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}J_{s-\varepsilon^{\gamma},s+\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}^{\varepsilon}\delta_{0}({\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon}})\delta_{0}({\omega_{u}-\omega_{v}})|\Phi|\right].

Moreover, we can see from Appendix
A.4
that there exists
\delta\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\int_{T^{3}_{s,u,v}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}J^{\varepsilon}_{0,s+\varepsilon^{\gamma};s-\varepsilon^{\gamma},s+\varepsilon^{\gamma}}\delta_{0}({\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon}})\delta_{0}({\omega_{u}-\omega_{v}})|\Phi|\right]=O(\varepsilon^{\delta-1})

(3.22)

\displaystyle\int_{T^{3}_{s,u,v}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}J^{\varepsilon}_{s-\varepsilon^{\gamma},s+\varepsilon^{\gamma};s-\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}\delta_{0}({\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon}})\delta_{0}({\omega_{u}-\omega_{v}})|\Phi|\right]=O(\varepsilon^{\delta-1}).

(3.23)

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}sI_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1)}

\displaystyle=\left(1+O\left(\varepsilon^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right)\int_{T^{3}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\delta_{0}(\omega_{u}-\omega_{v})\Phi\right]

(3.24)

\displaystyle\quad+\max|f|O(\varepsilon^{\widetilde{\delta}-1}).

for some
\widetilde{\delta}>0
.

Also, we can see that

\displaystyle\int_{T^{3}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\delta_{0}(\omega_{u}-\omega_{v})\Phi\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{T^{3}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}vE\left[e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\delta_{0}(\omega_{u}-\omega_{v})\left(\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}+\Phi_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}+\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\max|f|O(\varepsilon^{\gamma-1})

\displaystyle=\int_{T^{3}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\iint\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}yE\left[e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(\omega_{s}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon})\delta_{0}(\omega_{u}-\omega_{v})\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0001pt\left.\phantom{e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}}\times\delta_{0}(x-\omega_{s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}})\delta_{0}({y}-\omega_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma}})\left(\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}+\Phi_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}+\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\max|f|O(\varepsilon^{\gamma-1})

\displaystyle=\int_{T^{3}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\iint\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}yE\left[\left.e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(x-\omega_{s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 70.0001pt\left.\left.\phantom{e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}}\times\left(\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}+\Phi_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}+\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}\right)\right|\omega_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma}}=y\right]q_{u,s,v}(x,y)

\displaystyle\quad+\max|f|O(\varepsilon^{\gamma-1}),

(3.25)

where

\displaystyle q_{u,s,v}(x,y)

\displaystyle:=\int\text{\rm d}z\int\text{\rm d}wp_{u-(s-\varepsilon^{\gamma})}(x,z)p_{s-u}(z,w)p_{v-s}(w,z)p_{s+\varepsilon-v}(z,w)p_{(s+\varepsilon^{\gamma})-(s+\varepsilon)}(w,y).

Explicit calculation implies

\displaystyle q_{u,s,v}(x,y)=\tau p_{2\varepsilon^{\gamma}-\varepsilon-(s-u)+\sigma}(x,y),

where

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}((s+\varepsilon-v)(v-s)+(s+\varepsilon-v)(s-u)+(v-s)(s-u))^{-\frac{3}{2}},

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle:=\frac{(s+\varepsilon-v)(v-s)(s-u)}{(s+\varepsilon-v)(v-s)+(s+\varepsilon-e)(s-u)+(v-s)(s-u)}\leq s-u.

In particular, we have
2\varepsilon^{\gamma}-\varepsilon^{\beta}-\varepsilon\leq 2\varepsilon^{\gamma}-\varepsilon-(s-u)+\sigma\leq 2\varepsilon^{\gamma}-\varepsilon
.
Let

\displaystyle r_{u,s,,v}(x,y):=q_{u,s,v}(x,y)-\tau p_{2\varepsilon^{\gamma}}(x,y).

Then, we derive in a similar way to (
3.22
)-(
3.23
) that

\displaystyle\int_{T^{3}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\iint\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}yE\left[\left.e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(x-\omega_{s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 50.00008pt\left.\left.\phantom{e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}}\times\left(\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}+\Phi_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}+\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}\right)\right|\omega_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma}}=y\right]\tau p_{2\varepsilon^{\gamma}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\int_{T^{3}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\tau E\left[e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\left(\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}+\Phi_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}+\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{T^{3}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\tau E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\Phi_{0,1}\right]+O\left(\varepsilon^{\gamma-\frac{3}{2}}\right)\max|f|

(3.26)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\text{\rm d}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\rho(\varepsilon)+O\left(\varepsilon^{\gamma-\frac{3}{2}}\right)\max|f|,

(3.27)

where we have used (
A.9
) in the last line.
For the proof of (
3.26
), we note that from (
3.21
) we have

\displaystyle E\left[e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\left(\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}+\Phi_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}+\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\left(\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}+\Phi_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}+\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+O\left(E\left[e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\left|\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}\right|\left(\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}+\Phi_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}+\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}\right)\right]\right)

where we have used the fact that

\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}\leq 2\lambda\varepsilon^{\gamma}\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon)-\lambda^{2}\varepsilon^{\gamma}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)\leq 1
for
\varepsilon>0
small enough
and
\left|e^{x}-e^{y}\right|\leq e^{x+1}|x-y|
for
x+1>y
. Then, it is easy to see that the last is
O\left(\varepsilon^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\max|f|
and the first term is
E\left[e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\Phi\right]+O(\varepsilon^{\gamma})\max|f|
.

Also, we show that

\displaystyle\int_{T^{3}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\iint\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}yE\left[\left.e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(x-\omega_{s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 50.00008pt\left.\left.\phantom{e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}}\times\left(\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}+\Phi_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}+\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}\right)\right|\omega_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma}}=y\right]r_{u,s,v}(x,y)

\displaystyle=O\left(\varepsilon^{\beta-\gamma-1}\right)\max|f|.

(3.28)

Indeed, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\left|q_{u,s,v}(x,y)-\tau p_{2\varepsilon^{\gamma}}(x,y)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\tau\frac{s+\varepsilon-u-\sigma}{2\varepsilon^{\gamma}-\varepsilon-(s-u)+\sigma}p_{2\varepsilon^{\gamma}-\varepsilon-(s-u)+\sigma}(x,y)

\displaystyle\quad+C\tau\frac{(s+\varepsilon-u-\sigma)|x-y|^{2}}{(2\varepsilon^{\gamma}-\varepsilon-(s-u)+\sigma)(2\varepsilon^{\gamma})}p_{2\varepsilon^{\gamma}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\beta-\gamma}\tau\left(1+\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{\varepsilon^{\gamma}}\right)p_{2\varepsilon^{\gamma}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=:\widetilde{R}_{u,s,v}(x,y)

and
\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}\geq\overline{J}_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}+\overline{J}_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}
.
Therefore, in view of Lemma
3.1
we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{T^{3}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\iint\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}yE\left[\left.e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(x-\omega_{s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}})\right.\right.\right.

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 40.00006pt\left.\phantom{\int}\left.\left.\phantom{e^{-\check{J}_{0,1}^{s,\varepsilon,\lambda}}}\times\left(\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}+\Phi_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}+\Phi_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma};s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}\right)\right|\omega_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma}}=y\right]r_{u,s,v}(x,y)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max|f|\int_{T^{3}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\quad\times\iint\text{\rm d}x\text{\rm d}yE\left[\left.e^{-\overline{J}_{0,s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}}\delta_{0}(x-\omega_{s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}})\right|\omega_{s+\varepsilon^{\gamma}}=y\right]\widetilde{R}_{u,s,v}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|\varepsilon^{\beta-\gamma}\int_{T^{3}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\tau

\displaystyle\leq O(\varepsilon^{\beta-\gamma-1})\max|f|.

Thus, we obtain from (
3.24
)-(
3.28
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}sI_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,1)}=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)\rho(\varepsilon)+\max|f|O(\varepsilon^{\widetilde{\delta}-1})

for some
\widetilde{\delta}\in(0,1)
.

Similarly, we can prove that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}sI_{\varepsilon,s}^{(2,2)}=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)\rho(\varepsilon)+\max|f|O(\varepsilon^{\widetilde{\delta}-1})

for some
\widetilde{\delta}\in(0,1)
.

4
Some estimates for the self-intersection local times with respect to
\nu_{\lambda}

In Section
2
, we actually proved that
\left\{\widehat{J}_{2^{-\epsilon_{0}n},2^{-n}}(u_{1},u_{2},h)\right\}_{n\geq 1}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{p}(\nu_{0})
for
1\leq p\leq 4
. The aim of this section is to prove that this sequence is also Cauchy in
L^{1}(\nu_{\lambda})
for
\lambda\in(0,\infty)
. Lemma
3.2
will help us to achieve it. The main result in this section is the following theorem, which will play an important role in the proof that
\nu_{\lambda}
is
h
-quasi-invariant for
h\in K_{0}
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\gamma\in\left(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2}\right)
be the constant in Lemma
2.3
. Then, there exists
\lambda_{0}\in(0,\infty)
such that the followings hold.

(i)

For any given
u_{1},u_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\lambda\in(0,\lambda_{0})
,
1\leq p<4
and
h\in K
(with
K
as in Section
1.3
), there is a random variable
\rho(u_{1},u_{2},h)\in L^{p}(\nu_{\lambda})
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}E_{\nu_{\lambda}}\left[\left|\widehat{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u_{1},u_{2},h)-\rho(u_{1},u_{2},h)\right|^{p}\right]=0

(
\widehat{J}
is defined between Corollary
2.2
and Lemma
2.3
).

(ii)

For any given
u_{1},u_{2}\in[-M,M]
(
M>0
),
\lambda\in(0,\lambda_{0})
,
h\in K
, and
1\leq p<4
, there is a constant
C\in(0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle E_{\nu_{\lambda}}\left[\left|\rho(u_{1},u_{2},h)\right|^{p}\right]\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{\gamma p}.

Remark 4.2
.

When
u_{1}=u
and
u_{2}=0
, we have that

\displaystyle\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u,h)\to\rho(u,0,h)=:\rho(u,h)\quad\mathrm{in~}L^{p}(\nu_{\lambda})

as
n\to\infty
(
\widetilde{J}
was defined just after Corollary
2.2
).

Remark 4.3
.

Compared with
[
23
]
, we only discuss the case for small
\lambda
and
1\leq p<4
. This is based on Lemma
2.3
.

Proof.

For
u_{1},u_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
h\in K
, we let

\displaystyle\Phi_{n}=\Phi_{n}(u_{1},u_{2},h):=\widehat{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u_{1},u_{2},h)-\widehat{J}_{\varepsilon_{n-1},a_{n-1}}(u_{1},u_{2},h),\quad n\in{\mathbb{N}}.

(4.1)

It follows that

\displaystyle\Phi_{n}

\displaystyle=\int_{T_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cap\Delta_{0,1,0,1}}\text{\rm d}\sigma\text{\rm d}\tau\int e^{i\langle y,\omega_{\sigma}-\omega_{\tau}\rangle}\left(e^{i\langle y,u_{1}(h_{\sigma}-h_{\tau})\rangle}-e^{i\langle y,u_{2}(h_{\sigma}-h_{\tau})\rangle}\right)f_{a_{n}}(y)\text{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt-\int_{T_{\varepsilon_{n-1}}\cap\Delta_{0,1,0,1}}\text{\rm d}\sigma\text{\rm d}\tau\int e^{i\langle y,\omega_{\sigma}-\omega_{\tau}\rangle}\left(e^{i\langle y,u_{1}(h_{\sigma}-h_{\tau})\rangle}-e^{i\langle y,u_{2}(h_{\sigma}-h_{\tau})\rangle}\right)f_{a_{n-1}}(y)\text{\rm d}y.

Hence,
\Phi_{n}
has the form
\Phi^{1}-\Phi^{2}
, where
\Phi^{1}
and
\Phi^{2}
are functions defined by (
3.5
) with replacement of
f
by
\widetilde{f}_{1}
and
\widetilde{f}_{2}
, respectively (see Remark
3.3
). Since
|e^{ix}-e^{iy}|\leq|x-y|
, there exists a constant
C>0
which is independent of
\epsilon_{0}
such that
\max|\widetilde{f}_{1}|+\max|\widetilde{f}_{2}|\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|2^{2n}
.
Therefore, Lemma
3.2
and Remark
3.3
imply that

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{1},\lambda}\right)|\Phi_{n}|^{p}\right]-E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{2},\lambda}\right)|\Phi_{n}|^{p}\right]\right|\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{p}2^{2pn}(\varepsilon_{1}^{\widetilde{\delta}}+\varepsilon_{2}^{\widetilde{\delta}}),

\displaystyle\hskip 280.00043pt\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in(0,1).

Letting
\varepsilon_{1}\to 0
, in view of (
1.2
) we have that for
\varepsilon\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\left|N(\lambda)E_{\nu_{\lambda}}\left[|\Phi_{n}|^{p}\right]-E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right)|\Phi_{n}|^{p}\right]\right|\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{p}2^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}2pn}}\varepsilon^{\widetilde{\delta}}.

(4.2)

We choose

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n}=2^{-{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}3}p\widetilde{\delta}^{-1}n}.

(4.3)

Then, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|N(\lambda)E_{\nu_{\lambda}}\left[|\Phi_{n}|^{p}\right]-E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n},\lambda}\right)|\Phi_{n}|^{p}\right]\right|\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{p}2^{-pn}.

(4.4)

By Lemma
2.3
, we know that there is a constant
\widetilde{\delta}^{\prime}\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle E\left[|\Phi_{n}|^{pq}\right]\leq C_{p}|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{pq\gamma}2^{-pq\widetilde{\delta}^{\prime}n}

for
q\geq 1
with
pq\leq 4
.
From the definition of
\overline{J}_{u,v}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}
, we see that for
q\geq 1
and
0\leq u<v\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{q}{q-1}\overline{J}_{u,v}^{{\varepsilon},\lambda}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{u,v}^{{\varepsilon},\frac{q}{q-1}\lambda}+\left(\frac{q^{2}}{(q-1)^{2}}-\frac{q}{q-1}\right)\lambda^{2}(v-u)\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{u,v}^{{\varepsilon},\frac{q}{q-1}\lambda}\right)\exp\left(\frac{q\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}({\varepsilon})}{(q-1)^{2}}(v-u)\right).

(4.5)

This together with (
3.1
) implies that

\displaystyle E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n},\lambda}\right)|\Phi_{n}|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq E\left[\exp\left(-\frac{q}{q-1}\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]^{\frac{q-1}{q}}E\left[|\Phi_{n}|^{pq}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{p\gamma}2^{-p\widetilde{\delta}^{\prime}n}\exp\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n})}{q-1}\right)E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n},\frac{q\lambda}{q-1}}\right)\right]^{\frac{q-1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{p\gamma}2^{-p\widetilde{\delta}^{\prime}n}\exp\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n})}{q-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{p\gamma}2^{-\frac{p\widetilde{\delta}^{\prime}n}{2}},

(4.6)

if
\lambda>0
is small enough such that
\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n})}{q-1}<\frac{p\widetilde{\delta}^{\prime}n}{2}\log 2
.
By (
4.4
) and (
4.6
), we then have that
