\displaystyle N(\lambda)E_{\nu_{\lambda}}[|\Phi_{n}|^{p}]\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{p\gamma}\left(2^{-pn}+2^{-\frac{p\widetilde{\delta}^{\prime}n}{2}}\right),

which proves that the sequence
(\widehat{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u_{1},u_{2},h))_{n\geq 1}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{p}(\nu_{\lambda})
. Then, there exists a
\rho(u_{1},u_{2},h)
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}E_{\nu_{\lambda}}\left[\left|\widehat{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u_{1},u_{2},h)-\rho(u_{1},u_{2},h)\right|^{p}\right]=0

and assertion (i) is proved.

From (i) and (
3.4
) we see that

\displaystyle N(\lambda)E_{\nu_{\lambda}}\left[|\rho(u_{1},u_{2},h)|^{p}\right]^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq N(\lambda)E_{\nu_{\lambda}}\left[|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{1},a_{1}}(u_{1},h)-\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{1},a_{1}}(u_{2},h)|^{p}\right]^{1/p}+\sum_{n\geq 2}N(\lambda)E_{\nu_{\lambda}}\left[|\Phi_{n}|^{p}\right]^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq C|u_{1}-u_{2}|^{\gamma}.

Thus, we obtain (ii).

By a similar argument to the proof of Theorem
4.1
, we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 4.4
.

Suppose that random variable
\Phi_{n}
satisfies
E[|\Phi_{n}|^{p}]\leq C2^{-bn}
for some
b>0
and
p>1
. We set
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n}=2^{-cn}
for some
c>0
.
If
\frac{c}{(p-1)(2\pi)^{2}}\lambda^{2}<\frac{b}{2p}
, then

\displaystyle E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{n},\lambda}\right)\left|\Phi_{n}\right|\right]\leq C2^{-\widetilde{\delta}n}

for some
C>0
and
\widetilde{\delta}>0
.

5
Proof of Theorem
1.7

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{n,\lambda}

\displaystyle:=\lambda J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}-\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon_{n})+\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon_{n}),

\displaystyle\mu_{n,\lambda}

\displaystyle:=E\left[\exp\left(-\mathcal{J}_{n,\lambda}^{\prime}\right)\right]^{-1}\exp\left(-\mathcal{J}_{n,\lambda}^{\prime}\right)\nu_{0},

where
\mathcal{J}_{n,\lambda}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{J}_{n,\lambda}1\left\{\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|\leq 2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}\right\}
for
\widetilde{\delta}_{1}\in(0,\frac{1}{200})
which is chosen later.

In this section,
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
will be retaken and made smaller again and again, but only finitely many times.
Let us first prove the following two lemmas.

Lemma 5.1
.

For
\lambda\in[0,\infty)
small enough and for
\widetilde{\delta}_{1}\in\left(0,\frac{1}{200}\right)
small enough,

\displaystyle\mu_{n,\lambda}\Rightarrow\nu_{\lambda}\quad\text{in the weak sense as $n\to\infty$}.

Proof.

We note that there is a constant
\widetilde{c}\in(0,\infty)
which is independent of
\lambda
and
\epsilon_{0}
such that

\displaystyle E\left[\exp\left(-2\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},2\lambda}+2\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon_{n})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{2\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon_{n})}\leq C2^{2\widetilde{c}\lambda^{2}\epsilon_{0}n}.

(5.1)

Let
\Xi
be a nonnegative measurable function. Then, for any given
\lambda\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\Xi\exp\left(-\mathcal{J}_{n,\lambda}^{\prime}\right)\right]-E\left[\Xi\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda 2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}E\left[\Xi\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]+E\left[\Xi\left(1+\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right):\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|\geq 2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}\right]

(5.2)

as long as
\lambda 2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}\leq 1
is sufficiently small.
Taking
\Xi=\Psi_{0,1}
which is defined as in (
3.2
), we can see from Corollary
2.2
, Lemma
3.1
, (
5.1
) and (
5.2
) that

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\Psi_{0,1}\exp\left(-\mathcal{J}_{n,\lambda}^{\prime}\right)\right]-E\left[\Psi_{0,1}\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq C2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}+C2^{\left(\widetilde{c}\lambda^{2}\epsilon_{0}-\frac{1}{6}+\widetilde{\delta_{1}}\right)n}+C2^{\left(\widetilde{c}\lambda^{2}\epsilon_{0}-\frac{1+20\gamma}{24}+\frac{3\epsilon_{0}}{4}+\widetilde{\delta}_{1}\right)n}\leq C2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{2}n}

(5.3)

for some
\widetilde{\delta}_{2}\in(0,1)
if
\lambda
and
\widetilde{\delta}_{1}
are sufficiently small, where we have used the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in the second inequality.
By definition of
\nu_{\lambda}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to 0}E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]^{-1}E\left[\Psi_{0,1}\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]=E_{\nu_{\lambda}}\left[\Psi_{0,1}\right].

It follows from (
5.3
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to 0}E\left[\exp\left(-\mathcal{J}_{n,\lambda}^{\prime}\right)\right]^{-1}E\left[\Psi_{0,1}\exp\left(-\mathcal{J}_{n,\lambda}^{\prime}\right)\right]=E_{\nu_{\lambda}}\left[\Psi_{0,1}\right].

Let

\displaystyle A_{n}:=\left\{\omega:\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\omega)-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\omega)\right|<2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}\right\}

and let
\widetilde{\delta}_{1}\in(0,\frac{1}{200})
be the small constant which is given in Lemma
5.1
and will be retaken and made smaller in the proof of Lemma
5.2
below.
Set

\displaystyle D_{n,\lambda}(\omega):=\exp\left(-\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{n,\lambda}(\omega-uh)+\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{n,\lambda}(\omega)\right)\exp\left(u\int_{0}^{1}h_{t}^{\prime}\text{\rm d}\omega_{t}-\frac{1}{2}u^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(h_{t}^{\prime})^{2}\text{\rm d}t\right)

with
\int_{0}^{1}h_{t}^{\prime}\text{\rm d}\omega_{t}
understood as an Itô stochastic integral. It is clear that
D_{n,\lambda}=\frac{\text{\rm d}(\mu_{n,\lambda}\circ\tau_{uh}^{-1})}{\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}}
. Since we do not know at present whether
(\omega_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
, as coordinate process under
\nu_{\lambda}
, is a semimartingale or not, the quantity
\int_{0}^{1}h^{\prime}_{t}\text{\rm d}\omega_{t}
is not guaranteed to be well-defined under the polymer measure
\nu_{\lambda}
.
Moreover, it is known that
\nu_{\lambda}
is singular with respect to
\nu_{0}
(see
[
124
]
).
However, if
h\in K_{0}
, by Itô’s formula we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}h^{\prime}_{t}\text{\rm d}\omega_{t}=h^{\prime}_{1}\omega_{1}-\int_{0}^{1}\omega_{t}\text{\rm d}h^{\prime}_{t}.

(5.4)

The right-hand side of the above equality is well-defined under the measure
\nu_{\lambda}
.
From now on we assume that
h\in K_{0}
, and we always regard the left-hand side of (
5.4
) under the measure
\nu_{\lambda}
as the term defined by the right-hand side of (
5.4
).
Let

\displaystyle a_{uh}:=e^{-\lambda\rho(-u,h)+V(u,h)},

where

\displaystyle V(u,h)=V(\omega,u,h):=uh^{\prime}_{1}\omega_{1}-u\int_{0}^{1}\omega_{t}h^{\prime\prime}_{t}\text{\rm d}t-\frac{1}{2}u^{2}\int_{0}^{1}|h_{t}^{\prime}|^{2}\text{\rm d}t,

and
\rho(u,h)
is as defined in Theorem
4.1
.

Lemma 5.2
.

For any given
u\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
h\in K_{0}
and for
\lambda\in[0,\infty)
small enough, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\text{\rm d}{(\nu_{\lambda}\circ\tau_{{uh}}^{-1})}}{\text{\rm d}\nu_{\lambda}}=a_{uh},\quad\nu_{\lambda}\text{-a.e.}

This lemma follows from the following lemma.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{0}
be the set of bounded continuous functions
f:\mathbb{R}^{3p}\to{\mathbb{R}}
for some
p\geq 1
. Then, for any given
u\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
h\in K_{0}
and for
\lambda\in[0,\infty)
small enough, the following holds

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int f(\omega_{t_{1}},\dots,\omega_{t_{p}})\frac{\text{\rm d}(\mu_{n,\lambda}\circ\tau_{uh}^{-1})}{\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}}\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}=\int f(\omega_{t_{1}},\dots,\omega_{t_{p}})a_{uh}\text{\rm d}\nu_{\lambda}

(5.5)

for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}
and any
0\leq t_{1}<\dots<t_{p}\leq 1
with
p\geq 1
.

Proof of Lemma
5.2
.

By lemma
5.1
, we also know that for any given
u\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
h\in K_{0}

\displaystyle\mu_{n,\lambda}\circ\tau_{uh}^{-1}\Rightarrow\nu_{\lambda}\circ\tau_{uh}^{-1}\,\,\textrm{ in the weak sense},

as
n\to\infty
.
Hence, we obtain that for any given
f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}
and
0\leq t_{1}<\dots<t_{p}\leq 1
(
p\geq 1
)

\displaystyle\int f(\omega_{t_{1}},\dots,\omega_{t_{p}})\frac{\text{\rm d}(\mu_{n,\lambda}\circ\tau_{uh}^{-1})}{\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}}\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}\to\int f(\omega_{t_{1}},\dots,\omega_{t_{p}})\text{\rm d}(\nu_{\lambda}\circ\tau_{uh}^{-1}),

(5.6)

as
n\to\infty
.
Combining (
5.5
) and (
5.6
), we have that

\displaystyle\int f(\omega_{t_{1}},\dots,\omega_{t_{p}})\text{\rm d}(\nu_{\lambda}\circ\tau_{uh}^{-1})

\displaystyle=\int f(\omega_{t_{1}},\dots,\omega_{t_{p}})a_{uh}\text{\rm d}\nu_{\lambda},

\displaystyle\textrm{for all\, }f\in\mathcal{F}_{0},0\leq t_{1}<\dots<t_{p}\leq 1

This implies that both
\text{\rm d}\left(\nu_{\lambda}\circ\tau_{uh}^{-1}\right)
and
a_{uh}\text{\rm d}\nu_{\lambda}
are the probability measures on the Wiener space and that their finite-dimensional distributions coincide.
Therefore, we obtain the desired result.

We postpone the proof of Lemma
5.3
to Section
5.1
, and now prove Theorem
1.7
.

Proof of Theorem
1.7
.

We have to show that for each
h\in K_{0}
, there is
\{a_{uh}^{\prime},u\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
such that

\displaystyle\nu_{\lambda}(a_{uh}^{\prime}=a_{uh})=1,\quad u\in R,

(5.7)

\displaystyle\nu_{\lambda}(a_{uh}^{\prime}\text{ is continuous with respect to $u\in{\mathbb{R}}$})=1.

(5.8)

We choose a small
\lambda>0
so that the assertions in Theorem
4.1
, and Lemmas
5.1
and
5.2
hold.
By Lemma
5.2
, we know that
a_{uh}=\frac{\text{\rm d}\tau_{uh}(\nu_{\lambda})}{\text{\rm d}\nu_{\lambda}}
,
\nu_{\lambda}
-almost everywhere.
By Theorem
4.1
and the Kolmogorov theorem, we know that there is
\rho^{\prime}(u,h)
such that

\displaystyle\nu_{\lambda}(\rho^{\prime}(u,h)=\rho(u,h))=1,\quad u\in{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\nu_{\lambda}(\rho^{\prime}(u,h)\text{ is continuous with respect to $u\in R$})=1.

For
h\in K_{0}
, we set

\displaystyle a_{uh}^{\prime}=e^{-\lambda\rho^{\prime}(-u,h)+V(u,h)}.

Then,
\{a_{uh}^{\prime};u\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
satisfies (
5.7
) and (
5.8
).

5.1
Proof of Lemma
5.3

We prove Lemma
5.3
. Hereafter, we fix
f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}
and
0\leq t_{1}<\dots<t_{p}\leq 1
.
Let

\displaystyle m(n)=\frac{\widetilde{\delta}\epsilon_{0}}{2}n,

where
\widetilde{\delta}>0
is a constant given in Lemma
3.2
.
For the proof of Lemma
5.3
, we prepare following lemmas.

Lemma 5.4
.

For any given
u\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
h\in K_{0}
and for
\lambda\in(0,\infty)
small enough

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}E_{\mu_{n,\lambda}}\left[\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u,h)\right|\right]<\infty.

(5.9)

Proof.

We find from Lemma
2.3
and (
5.1
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u,h)-\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{m(n)},a_{m(n)}}(u,h)\right|\right]\leq C

(5.10)

for
\lambda>0
small enough.
Moreover, we will see later that

\displaystyle\left\{E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{m(n)},a_{m(n)}}(u,h)\right|\right]\right\}\text{ is a Cauchy sequence.}

(5.11)

Combining (
5.10
) and (
5.11
) yields

\displaystyle E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u,h)\right|\right]\leq C\quad.

(5.12)

Taking
\Xi=|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u,h)|
in (
5.2
) and recalling that
\widetilde{\delta}_{1}\in(0,\frac{1}{200})
, we see from Hölder’s inequality that

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\exp\left(-\mathcal{J}_{n,\lambda}^{\prime}\right)\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u,h)\right|\right]-E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u,h)\right|\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda 2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}e^{\lambda 2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}}E\left[\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u,h)\right|\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+E\left[\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u,h)\right|\left(1+\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right):\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|\geq 2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}e^{\lambda 2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}}E\left[\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u,h)\right|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}E\left[\exp\left(-2\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+CE\left[\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u,h)\right|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(1+E\left[\exp\left(-4\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\right)\cdot 2^{\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}E\left[\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{3}n}

for some
\widetilde{\delta}_{3}>0
by Corollaries
2.2
and
2.4
, and (
4.5
), if
\lambda>0
small enough.
Hence, we can obtain (
5.9
) for small
\lambda>0
by (
5.12
).

We complete the proof by showing (
5.11
). We have

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{m(n)},a_{m(n)}}(u,h)\right|\right]-E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n-1},\lambda}\right)\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{m(n-1)},a_{m(n-1)}}(u,h)\right|\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{m(n)},a_{m(n)}}(u,h)\right|\right]-E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n-1},\lambda}\right)\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{m(n)},a_{m(n)}}(u,h)\right|\right]\right|

\displaystyle\quad+E\left[\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n-1},\lambda}\right)\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{m(n)},a_{m(n)}}(u,h)-\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{m(n-1)},a_{m(n-1)}}(u,h)\right|\right]

\displaystyle=:P_{n}^{1}+P_{n}^{2}.

Then, we see from Corollaries
2.2
,
2.4
and
4.4
that there exist
C>0
and
\widetilde{\delta}_{3}\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle P_{n}^{2}\leq 2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{3}n}

for
n\geq 1
if
\lambda>0
is sufficiently small.
Noting the definition of
m(n)
and applying Lemma
3.2
(see Remark
3.3
) to
P_{n}^{1}
, we see that there exist
C>0
and
\widetilde{\delta}_{4}\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle P_{n}^{1}\leq C2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{4}n}

for
n\geq 1
.
Thus, we obtain (
5.11
).

For
M>1
large enough, we set

\displaystyle B_{n}(M):=

\displaystyle\left\{\left|\lambda\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(-u,h)\right|\geq\log M\right\}\cup\left\{|V(u,h)|\geq\log M\right\}.

Lemma 5.5
.

For any given
u\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
h\in K_{0}
and for
\lambda\in(0,\infty)
small enough, there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mu_{n,\lambda}\circ\tau_{uh}^{-1}(B_{n}(M))=\mu_{n,\lambda}\left(\{\omega+uh\in B_{n}(M)\}\right)\leq\frac{C}{\log M}

for any
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

From Lemma
3.1
we easily obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}E_{\mu_{n,\lambda}}\left[|\omega_{1}|+\left(\int_{0}^{1}|\omega_{t}|^{2}\text{\rm d}t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\leq C<\infty.

(5.13)

Since it holds that

\displaystyle V(\omega+uh,u,h)=uh_{1}^{\prime}\omega_{1}-u\int_{0}^{1}\omega_{t}h_{t}^{\prime\prime}\text{\rm d}t+\frac{u^{2}}{2}\int_{0}^{1}|h_{t}^{\prime}|^{2}\text{\rm d}t+u^{2}h_{0}h^{\prime}_{0},

from (
5.13
) we have

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\mu_{n,\lambda}\left(\left|V(\cdot+uh,u,h)\right|\geq\log M\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{n\geq 1}\mu_{n,\lambda}\left(\left|uh^{\prime}_{1}\omega_{1}-u\int_{0}^{1}\omega_{t}h^{\prime\prime}_{t}\text{\rm d}t\right|+\frac{u^{2}}{2}\left|\int_{0}^{1}|h_{t}^{\prime}|^{2}\text{\rm d}t+h_{0}h^{\prime}_{0}\right|\geq\log M\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\log M}.

(5.14)

From Lemma
5.4
we also have

\displaystyle\mu_{n,\lambda}\circ\tau_{uh}^{-1}\left(\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(-u,h)\right|\geq\log M\right)=\mu_{n,\lambda}\left(\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(u,h)\right|\geq\log M\right)\leq\frac{C}{\log M}

(5.15)

for
n\geq 1
.
The assertion follows from (
5.14
) and (
5.15
).

Lemma 5.6
.

For any
R>0
, any given
v\in[-R,R]
,
h\in K_{0}
and for
\lambda\in(0,\infty)
small enough, there exist
C>0
and
\widetilde{\delta}_{5}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mu_{n,\lambda}\circ\tau_{vh}^{-1}(A_{n}^{c})=\mu_{n,\lambda}(\{\omega+vh\in A_{n}^{c}\})\leq{C2^{-{\widetilde{\delta}_{5}n}}}

(5.16)

for
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

Taking
\Xi=\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\omega+uh)-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\omega+uh)\right|
in (
5.2
), we have

\displaystyle E\left[\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\cdot+uh)-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\cdot+uh)\right|\exp\left(-\mathcal{J}_{n,\lambda}^{\prime}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq E\left[\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\cdot+uh)-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\cdot+uh)\right|\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\left|E\left[\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\cdot+uh)-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\cdot+uh)\right|\exp\left(-\mathcal{J}_{n,\lambda}^{\prime}\right)\right]\right.

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 85.35826pt\left.-E\left[\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\cdot+uh)-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\cdot+uh)\right|\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq E\left[\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\cdot+uh)-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\cdot+uh)\right|\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+2\lambda 2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}E\left[\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\cdot+uh)-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\cdot+uh)\right|\exp\left(-2\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+E\left[\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\cdot+uh)-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\cdot+uh)\right|\left(1+\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right)\right].

(5.17)

Then,
we see from Corollary
2.2
and (
5.1
) that there exists
\widetilde{\delta}_{4}>0
such that

the right-hand side of
(
5.17
)

\displaystyle\leq E\left[\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\cdot+uh)-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\cdot+uh)\right|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}E\left[\exp\left(-2\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda 2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{1}n}E\left[\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\cdot+uh)-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\cdot+uh)\right|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}E\left[\exp\left(-2\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+E\left[\left|J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\cdot+uh)-J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\cdot+uh)\right|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}E\left[\left(1+\exp\left(-\overline{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},\lambda}\right)\right)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{4}n}

for small
\lambda>0
.
Hence, there exist
C>0
and
\widetilde{\delta}_{5}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mu_{n,\lambda}(A_{n}^{c}(\cdot+uh))\leq{C2^{-{\widetilde{\delta}_{5}n}}},

for
n\geq 1
.

For
A\in\mathcal{B}
,
h\in K_{0}
, we set
\{\omega+h\in A\}
by
A(\cdot-h)
.

Lemma 5.7
.

For
M>1
large enough, any given
u\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
h\in K_{0}
, and
\lambda\in(0,\infty)
small enough, there exist
C>0
and
\widetilde{\delta}_{6}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{n}^{c}(M)\cap\left(A_{n}(\cdot-uh)\cap A_{n}(\cdot)\right)^{c}}f(\omega)e^{-\lambda\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(-u,h)+V(u,h)}\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}\right|\leq C2^{-\widetilde{\delta}_{6}n}M^{2}\max|f|.

(5.18)

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{n}^{c}(M)\cap\left(A_{n}(\cdot-uh)\cap A_{n}(\cdot)\right)^{c}}f(\omega)e^{-\lambda\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(-u,h)+V(u,h)}\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max|f|M^{2}\left|\int_{\left(A_{n}(\cdot-uh)\cap A_{n}(\cdot)\right)^{c}}\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}\right|,

and hence, the statement follows from Lemma
5.6
.

Now we prove Lemma
5.3
by using Lemmas
5.5
,
5.6
and
5.7
.

Proof of Lemma
5.3
.

We note that
B_{n}^{c}(M)
can be written as

\displaystyle\left\{|V(u,h)|\leq{\log M}\right\}\cap\left\{\left|\lambda\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(-u,h)\right|\leq\log M\right\}.

Let
\varphi_{M}\in C_{b}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
be a continuous function which satisfies

\displaystyle 0\leq\varphi_{M}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq 1,\quad

\displaystyle\textrm{if }(x_{1},x_{2})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},

\displaystyle\varphi_{M}(x_{1},x_{2})=1,\quad

\displaystyle\textrm{for}\ |x_{1}|\leq\log M,\ |x_{2}|\leq\log M,

\displaystyle\varphi_{M}(x_{1},x_{2})=0,\quad

\displaystyle\textrm{for}\ |x_{1}|\geq 2\log M,\text{ or }\ |x_{2}|\geq 2\log M,

and we set
\widetilde{\varphi}_{M}(\omega):=\varphi_{M}(\lambda\widetilde{J}_{n}(-u,h),V(u,h))
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\int f(\omega)D_{n,\lambda}\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}=\int\widetilde{\varphi}_{M}(\omega)f(\omega)D_{n,\lambda}\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}+\int\left(1-\widetilde{\varphi}_{M}(\omega)\right)f(\omega)D_{n,\lambda}\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}.

for
f(\omega)=f(\omega_{t_{1}},\dots,\omega_{t_{p}})
.

It is easy to see from Lemma
5.5
that

\displaystyle\left|\int\left(1-\widetilde{\varphi}_{M}(\omega)\right)f(\omega)D_{n,\lambda}\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}\right|\leq\sup|f|\mu_{n,\lambda}\circ\tau_{uh}^{-1}\left(B_{n}(M)\right)\leq\frac{C}{\log M}\max|f|.

(5.19)

We remark that

\displaystyle D_{n,\lambda}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\lambda\widetilde{J}_{n}(-u,h)1_{A_{n}(\omega-uh)\cap A_{n}(\omega)}+V(u,h)\right)

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(-\left(\lambda{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\omega-uh)-\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon_{n})+\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon_{n})\right)1_{A_{n}(\omega-uh)\backslash A_{n}(\omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(\left(\lambda{J}_{0,1}^{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(\omega)-\lambda\kappa_{1}(\varepsilon_{n})+\lambda^{2}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon_{n})\right)1_{A_{n}(\omega)\backslash A_{n}(\omega-uh)}\right),

and hence on
A_{n}(\cdot-uh)\cap A_{n}(\cdot)
,

\displaystyle D_{n,\lambda}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\lambda\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(-u,h)+V(u,h)\right).

Applying Lemma
5.6
for
v=u,0
and Lemma
5.7
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\int\widetilde{\varphi}_{M}(\omega)f(\omega){D_{n,\lambda}}\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}-\int\widetilde{\varphi}_{M}(\omega)f(\omega)e^{-\lambda\widetilde{J}_{n}(-u,h)+V(u,h)}\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}\right|\to 0

(5.20)

as
n\to\infty
.

Let
F_{M}:=\varphi_{M}(x_{1},x_{2})e^{-x_{1}+x_{2}}f(x_{3},\cdots,x_{p+2})\in C_{b}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{p+2})
.
Note that
F_{M}
is Lipschitz continuous for each
M
.
Then, we can see from the same argument as the proof of (
4.6
) and Corollary
2.4
that

\displaystyle\left|\int\left(F_{M}\left(\lambda\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(-u,h),V(u,h),\omega_{t_{1}},\cdots,\omega_{t_{p}})\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\left.\hskip 113.81102pt-F_{M}\left(\lambda\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{m(n)},a_{m(n)}}(-u,h),V(u,h),\omega_{t_{1}},\cdots,\omega_{t_{p}})\right)\right)\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,f}\int\left|\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(-u,h)-\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{m(n)},a_{m(n)}}(-u,h)\right|\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}\to 0.

Also, a similar argument as in (
4.2
) and (
4.4
) yields that

\displaystyle\left|\int F_{M}\left(\lambda\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{m(n)},a_{m(n)}}(-u,h),V(u,h),\omega_{t_{1}},\cdots,\omega_{t_{p}})\right)\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\hskip 85.35826pt-\int F_{M}\left(\lambda\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{m(n)},a_{m(n)}}(-u,h),V(u,h),\omega_{t_{1}},\cdots,\omega_{t_{p}})\right)\text{\rm d}\nu_{\lambda}\right|\to 0.

Theorem
4.1
(see Remark
4.2
) implies that

\displaystyle\int F_{M}\left(\lambda\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{m(n)},a_{m(n)}}(-u,h),V(u,h),\omega_{t_{1}},\cdots,\omega_{t_{p}})\right)\text{\rm d}\nu_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\to\int F_{M}(\lambda\rho(-u,h),V(u,h),\omega_{t_{1}},\cdots,\omega_{t_{p}})\text{\rm d}\nu_{\lambda}.

Putting these together, we obtain

\displaystyle\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left|\int F_{M}\left(\lambda\widetilde{J}_{\varepsilon_{n},a_{n}}(-u,h),V(u,h),\omega_{t_{1}},\cdots,\omega_{t_{p}})\right))\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 85.35826pt\left.-\int F_{M}\left(\lambda\rho(-u,h),V(u,h),\omega_{t_{1}},\cdots,\omega_{t_{p}}\right)\text{\rm d}\nu_{\lambda}\right|=0.

This inequality, (
5.19
) and (
5.20
) yield

\displaystyle\varlimsup_{n\to\infty}\left|\int f(\omega_{t_{1}},\dots,\omega_{t_{p}})D_{n,\lambda}\text{\rm d}\mu_{n,\lambda}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 85.35826pt\left.-\int\widetilde{\varphi}_{M}(\omega)e^{-\lambda\rho(-u,h)+V(u,h)}f(\omega_{t_{1}},\dots,\omega_{t_{p}})\text{\rm d}\nu_{\lambda}\right|\leq\frac{C}{\log M}\sup|f|.

Since

\displaystyle\lim_{M\to\infty}\int\widetilde{\varphi}_{M}(\omega)e^{-\lambda\rho(-u,h)+V(u,h)}f(\omega_{t_{1}},\dots,\omega_{t_{p}})\text{\rm d}\nu_{\lambda}=\int e^{-\lambda\rho(-u,h)+V(u,h)}f(\omega_{t_{1}},\dots,\omega_{t_{p}})\text{\rm d}\nu_{\lambda},

we obtain Lemma
5.3
.

This implies that also Theorem
1.7
has a complete proof.

Appendix A
Some estimates of integrals

In this appendix, we give some calculations of integrals, that have been used in the course of various proofs given above.

A.1
Proof of (
3.17
)

Observe that

\displaystyle\iint_{0<s_{2}<s_{1}<\infty,0<t_{1}<t_{2}<1}\text{\rm d}s_{1}\text{\rm d}s_{2}\text{\rm d}t_{1}\text{\rm d}t_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 113.81102pt[(s_{1}-s_{2})(t_{2}-t_{1})(1-t_{2}+s_{2})+(s_{1}-s_{2}+t_{2}-t_{1})(1-t_{2})s_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 227.62204pt+(s_{1}-s_{2}+t_{2}-t_{1})(1-t_{2}+s_{2})t_{1}]^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=2\iint_{0<s_{2}<\infty,0<t_{1}<t_{2}<1}\text{\rm d}s_{2}\text{\rm d}t_{1}\text{\rm d}t_{2}\frac{1}{s_{2}+t_{2}(1-t_{2})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{(t_{2}-t_{1})((1-t_{2}+s_{2})t_{1}+(1-t_{2})s_{2})}}

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{0<s_{2}<\infty,0<t_{2}<1}\text{\rm d}s_{2}\text{\rm d}t_{2}\frac{1}{s_{2}+t_{2}(1-t_{2})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-t_{2}+s_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0<t_{2}<1}\text{\rm d}t_{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t_{2}(1-t_{2})}}<\infty,

where in the first inequality we have used

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b}\frac{1}{\sqrt{(x-s)(t-x)+u}}\text{\rm d}x=\arcsin\frac{b-\frac{(s+t)}{2}}{\sqrt{u+\frac{(t-s)^{2}}{4}}}-\arcsin\frac{a-\frac{(s+t)}{2}}{\sqrt{u+\frac{(t-s)^{2}}{4}}}

when
s<t
,
u>0
and
a,b\in[s,t]
.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\iint_{0<s_{2}<s_{1}<\infty,0<t_{2}<t_{1}<1}\text{\rm d}s_{1}\text{\rm d}s_{2}\text{\rm d}t_{1}\text{\rm d}t_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt[(t_{2}+s_{2})(s_{1}-s_{2})(t_{1}-t_{2})+(s_{1}-s_{2}+t_{1}-t_{2})s_{2}t_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt\phantom{\int}+(t_{2}+s_{2})(s_{1}-s_{2}+t_{1}-t_{2})(1-t_{1})]^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=2\iint_{0<s_{2}<\infty,0<t_{2}<t_{1}<1}\text{\rm d}s_{2}\text{\rm d}t_{1}\text{\rm d}t_{2}\frac{1}{s_{2}+t_{2}(1-t_{2})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{(t_{1}-t_{2})(s_{2}t_{2}+(s_{2}+t_{2})(1-t_{1}))}}

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{0<s_{2}<\infty,0<t_{2}<1}\text{\rm d}s_{2}\text{\rm d}t_{2}\frac{1}{s_{2}+t_{2}(1-t_{2})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{s_{2}+t_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0<t_{2}<1}\text{\rm d}t_{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t_{2}(1-t_{2})}}<\infty.

A.2
Proof of (
3.19
)

Observe that

\displaystyle E\left[Y_{s,s+\varepsilon}^{2}e^{-\overline{J}_{0,1-\varepsilon}}\Phi_{0,1-\varepsilon}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|\int_{\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<s<\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}<s+\varepsilon\\
\tau_{1}-\sigma_{1}\leq\varepsilon,\tau_{2}-\sigma_{2}\leq\varepsilon\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}\sigma_{1}\text{\rm d}\tau_{1}\text{\rm d}\sigma_{2}\text{\rm d}\tau_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0001pt((\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})(\tau_{1}-\sigma_{1})+(\tau_{1}-\sigma_{2})(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}))^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle+C\max|f|\iint_{\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<s<\tau_{2}<\tau_{1}<s+\varepsilon\\
\tau_{1}-\sigma_{1}\leq\varepsilon,\tau_{2}-\sigma_{2}\leq\varepsilon\end{smallmatrix}}\text{\rm d}\sigma_{1}\text{\rm d}\tau_{1}\text{\rm d}\sigma_{2}\text{\rm d}\tau_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0001pt((\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}+\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})(\tau_{2}-\sigma_{2}))^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=:Y_{1}+Y_{2}.

Taking the integral of
Y_{1}
in
\tau_{2}\in[\tau_{1},\sigma_{2}+\varepsilon]
, the integrand is given by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\tau_{1}-\sigma_{1})}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{(\tau_{1}-\sigma_{2})(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{(\sigma_{2}+\varepsilon-\tau_{1})(\tau_{1}-\sigma_{1})+(\tau_{1}-\sigma_{1})(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{(\tau_{1}-\sigma_{1})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{(\tau_{1}-\sigma_{2})(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})}}.

Taking integral in
\tau_{1}\in[s,\sigma_{1}+\varepsilon]
gives an upper bound

\displaystyle\frac{2C}{\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}}\arctan\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}}(\sqrt{\sigma_{1}+\varepsilon-\sigma_{2}}-\sqrt{s-\sigma_{2}})}{\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}+\sqrt{(\sigma_{1}+\varepsilon-\sigma_{2})(s-\sigma_{2})}}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})(s-\sigma_{2})}},

where we have used the integral

\displaystyle\int_{d}^{e}\frac{\text{\rm d}x}{(x+a)\sqrt{bx+c}}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{ab-c}}\arctan\frac{\sqrt{ab-c}(\sqrt{be+c}-\sqrt{bd+c})}{ab-c+\sqrt{(be+c)(bd+c)}}

(A.1)

when
ab-c>0
. Then, taking integrals in
s-\varepsilon<\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<s
, we obtain an upper bound of
Y_{1}
,
C\max|f|\varepsilon
.

The integral in
Y_{2}
in
\tau_{1}\in[\tau_{2},\sigma_{1}+\varepsilon]
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{2}{(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\tau_{2}-\sigma_{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}-\frac{2}{(\sigma_{2}+\varepsilon-\tau_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\tau_{2}-\sigma_{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{(\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\tau_{2}-\sigma_{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

Taking integral in
\tau_{2}\in[s,\sigma_{1}+\varepsilon]
gives an upper bound

\displaystyle\frac{4}{((\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1})(s-\sigma_{2}))^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Then, the integration in
s-\varepsilon<\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<s
gives an upper bound of
Y_{2}
,
C\max|f|\varepsilon
.

A.3
Proof of (
3.20
)

By Lemma
3.1
and the some argument as in (
3.16
), we can find

\displaystyle E\left[\int\int_{0\leq u\leq s\leq v\leq 1-\varepsilon,v-u\leq\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\delta(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})e^{-\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{v,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda J_{0,u;v,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}}|\Phi_{0,1}|J_{0,u;u,v}^{\varepsilon}e^{K_{\varepsilon,v-u,\lambda}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}v\int_{v-\varepsilon}^{s}\text{\rm d}u\int_{u}^{v}\text{\rm d}t\int_{0}^{t-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r((t-u)(u-r)+(v-t)(t-u)+(v-t)(u-r))^{-\frac{3}{2}}

(A.2)

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}v\int_{v-\varepsilon}^{s}\text{\rm d}u\int_{u}^{v}\text{\rm d}t\frac{1}{v-u}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon(v-u)-(t-u)^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}v\int_{v-\varepsilon}^{s}\text{\rm d}u\frac{1}{v-u}\arcsin\sqrt{\frac{v-u}{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|\varepsilon,

where we have used that
K_{\varepsilon,v-u,\lambda}\leq c
for some constant
c>0
if
v-u\leq\varepsilon
in (
A.2
).

The same calculation yields

\displaystyle E\left[\int\int_{0\leq u\leq s\leq v\leq 1-\varepsilon,v-u\leq\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\delta(\omega_{v}-\omega_{u})e^{-\overline{J}_{0,u}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\overline{J}_{v,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}-\lambda J_{0,u;v,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}}|\Phi_{0,1}|J_{u,v;v,1-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}e^{K_{\varepsilon,v-u,\lambda}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\max|f|\varepsilon.

A.4
Proofs of (
3.22
), (
3.23
)

For (
3.22
), the region

\displaystyle T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma}:={T^{3}_{s,u,v}(\varepsilon^{\alpha},\varepsilon^{\beta})}\times\{(\sigma,\tau):0<\sigma<s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},s-\varepsilon^{\gamma}<\tau<s+\varepsilon^{\gamma},\tau-\sigma>\varepsilon\}

is partitioned into 15 regions due to the order of
\{s,u,v,s+\varepsilon,\sigma,\tau\}
in
[0,s+\varepsilon^{\gamma}]
, denoting by
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(i)}
(
i=1,\dots,15
):

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(1)}=\{\sigma<\tau<u<s<v<s+\varepsilon\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(2)}=\{\sigma<u<\tau<s<v<s+\varepsilon\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(3)}=\{\sigma<u<s<\tau<v<s+\varepsilon\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(4)}=\{\sigma<u<s<v<\tau<s+\varepsilon\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(5)}=\{\sigma<u<s<v<s+\varepsilon<\tau\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(6)}=\{u<\sigma<\tau<s<v<s+\varepsilon\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(7)}=\{u<\sigma<s<\tau<v<s+\varepsilon\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(8)}=\{u<\sigma<s<v<\tau<s+\varepsilon\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(9)}=\{u<\sigma<s<v<s+\varepsilon<\tau\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(10)}=\{u<s<\sigma<\tau<v<s+\varepsilon\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(11)}=\{u<s<\sigma<v<\tau<s+\varepsilon\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(12)}=\{u<s<\sigma<v<s+\varepsilon<\tau\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(13)}=\{u<s<v<\sigma<\tau<s+\varepsilon\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(14)}=\{u<s<v<\sigma<s+\varepsilon<\tau\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma},

\displaystyle R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(15)}=\{u<s<v<s+\varepsilon<\sigma<\tau\}\cap T^{4}_{s,u,v,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,\gamma}.

We remark that
R^{(10)}
,
R^{(11)}
,
R^{(13)}
are empty sets due to the restriction of
\tau-\sigma\geq\varepsilon
.
Then, we will estimate the integrals

\displaystyle C\int_{R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(i)}}\text{\rm d}s\text{\rm d}u\text{\rm d}v\text{\rm d}\sigma\text{\rm d}\tau Q^{(i)}(s,u,v,\sigma,\tau,\varepsilon)^{-\frac{3}{2}},

(A.3)

where
Q^{(i)}(s,u,v,\sigma,\tau,\varepsilon)
is a polynomial function of
(s,u,v,\sigma,\tau,\varepsilon)
on
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(i)}
for each
i=1,\dots,15
.

For each region, we can find an associated electrical circuit given in figure
6
as follows:

•

(1):
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(5)}
.

•

(2):
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(2)}
,
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(3)}
,
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(4)}
,
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(7)}
,
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(8)}
,
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(9)}
,
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(11)}
,
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(12)}
,
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(14)}
.

•

(3):
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(1)}
,
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(15)}
.

•

(4):
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(6)}
,
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(10)}
,
R_{s,u,v,\sigma,\tau}^{(13)}
.

Figure 6:
Q^{(i)}
can be computed from these types of electrical circuits. The associated electrical circuit for
R^{(15)}
has the other version of (3) where the tail from

is adjusted to

. Even for such a version,

has the same form.

In particular,

has the form

\displaystyle\begin{cases}b(cd+de+ec)+f(cd+de+ec)+cde\quad&(1)\text{ in Figure }\ref{Fig:TypeCir},\\
bc(e+f)+ef(b+c)+d(b+c)(e+f)\quad&(2)\text{ in Figure }\ref{Fig:TypeCir},\\
b(de+ef+fd)\quad&(3)\text{ in Figure }\ref{Fig:TypeCir},\\
c((b+d)e+(b+d)f+ef)&(4)\text{ in Figure }\ref{Fig:TypeCir}.\end{cases}

Applying this to each case and simplifying the polynomial,
Q^{(i)}
is given as follows.

\displaystyle Q^{(1)}=(\tau-\sigma)((s+\varepsilon-v)(v-s)+\varepsilon(s-u)),

\displaystyle Q^{(2)}=(u-\sigma)(\tau-u)[(s+\varepsilon-v)+(v-s)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(v-s)(s+\varepsilon-v)[(\tau-u)+(u-\sigma)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(s-\tau)[(\tau-u)+(u-\sigma)][(s+\varepsilon-v)+(v-s)],

\displaystyle Q^{(3)}=(u-\sigma)(v-\tau)[(s+\varepsilon-v)+(s-u)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(s-u)(s+\varepsilon-v)[(v-\tau)+(u-\sigma)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(\tau-s)[(v-\tau)+(u-\sigma)][(s+\varepsilon-v)+(s-u)],

\displaystyle Q^{(4)}=(s+\varepsilon-\tau)[(\tau-v)+(u-\sigma)][(v-s)+(s-u)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(s-u)(v-s)[(\tau-v)+(u-\sigma)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(\tau-v)(u-\sigma)[(v-s)+(s-u)],

\displaystyle Q^{(5)}=(\tau-s-\varepsilon)[(s-u)(v-s)+(v-s)(s+\varepsilon-v)+(s+\varepsilon-v)(s-u)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(u-\sigma)[(s-u)(v-s)+(v-s)(s+\varepsilon-v)+(s+\varepsilon-v)(s-u)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(s-u)(v-s)(s+\varepsilon-v),

\displaystyle Q^{(6)}=(\tau-\sigma)((s+\varepsilon-v)(v-s)+\varepsilon(s-\tau+\sigma-u)),

\displaystyle Q^{(7)}=(s+\varepsilon-v)[(v-\tau)+(\sigma-u)][(\tau-s)+(s-\sigma)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(v-\tau)(\sigma-u)[(\tau-s)+(s-\sigma)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(\tau-s)(s-\sigma)[(v-\tau)+(\sigma-u)],

\displaystyle Q^{(8)}=(v-s)[(\tau-v)+(\sigma-u)][(s-\sigma)+(s+\varepsilon-\tau)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(\tau-v)(\sigma-u)[(s-\sigma)+(s+\varepsilon-\tau)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(s-\sigma)(s+\varepsilon-\tau)[(\tau-v)+(\sigma-u)],

\displaystyle Q^{(9)}=(\sigma-u)[(s-\sigma)+(\tau-s-\varepsilon)][(s+\varepsilon-v)+(v-s)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(s-\sigma)(\tau-s-\varepsilon)[(s+\varepsilon-v)+(v-s)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(s+\varepsilon-v)(v-s)[(s-\sigma)+(\tau-s-\varepsilon)],

\displaystyle Q^{(12)}=(v-\sigma)[(s+\varepsilon-v)+(s-u)][(\tau-s-\varepsilon)+(\sigma-s)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(s+\varepsilon-v)(s-u)[(\tau-s-\varepsilon)+(\sigma-s)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(\tau-s-\varepsilon)(\sigma-s)[(s+\varepsilon-v)+(s-u)],

\displaystyle Q^{(14)}=(\sigma-v)[(v-s)+(s-u)][(\tau-s-\varepsilon)+(s+\varepsilon-\sigma)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(s+\varepsilon-\sigma)(\tau-s-\varepsilon)[(v-s)+(s-u)]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+(v-s)(s-u)[(\tau-s-\varepsilon)+(s+\varepsilon-\sigma)],

\displaystyle Q^{(15)}=(\tau-\sigma)((s+\varepsilon-v)(v-s)+\varepsilon(s-u)).

Estimates of all integrals in (
A.3
) are obtained by explicit calculations.
By reversing the time, (
3.23
) can be reduced to (
3.22
).

A.5
Asymptotics of Expectation and Variance of
J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon}

In this subsection, we prove (
1.5
)-(
1.7
).

A.5.1
Proof (
1.5
)

Observe that

\displaystyle E\left[J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon}\right]=\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}tp_{t-s}(0)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{1}{(1-s)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\text{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left(\frac{1-\varepsilon}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}-2(1-\sqrt{\varepsilon})\right).

A.5.2
Proof of (
1.6
)

It is easy to see that

\displaystyle E\left[\left(J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=2\int_{\varepsilon}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\int_{t+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}u\int_{0}^{t-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{t}\text{\rm d}s\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\text{\rm d}x\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\text{\rm d}yp_{r}(0,x)p_{s-r}(x,x)p_{t-s}(x,y)p_{u-t}(y,y)

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\int_{t+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}u\int_{0}^{t}\text{\rm d}r\int_{(r+\varepsilon)\vee t}^{u}\text{\rm d}s\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\text{\rm d}x\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\text{\rm d}yp_{r}(0,x)p_{t-r}(x,y)p_{s-t}(y,x)p_{u-s}(x,y)

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\int_{t+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}u\int_{t}^{u-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{u}\text{\rm d}s\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\text{\rm d}x\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\text{\rm d}yp_{t}(0,x)p_{r-t}(x,y)p_{s-r}(y,y)p_{u-s}(y,x)

\displaystyle=:2V_{1}(\varepsilon)+2V_{2}(\varepsilon)+2V_{3}(\varepsilon).

We should now investigate the asymptotics of each term:

Observe that

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{3}V_{1}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\int_{\varepsilon}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\int_{t+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}u\int_{0}^{t-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{t}\text{\rm d}s(s-r)^{-\frac{3}{2}}(u-t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\varepsilon}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\int_{t+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}u(u-t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\int_{0}^{t-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}-(t-r)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\varepsilon}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\int_{t+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}u(u-t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left((t-\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}-2\sqrt{t}+2\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=4\int_{\varepsilon}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}-(1-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\left((t-\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}-2\sqrt{t}+2\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=4\int_{\varepsilon}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\left(\varepsilon^{-1}t-\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}t-2\sqrt{t}{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)+C_{1}(\varepsilon),

where
C_{1}(\varepsilon)
converges to some constant as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Since

\displaystyle\int_{\varepsilon}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\left(\varepsilon^{-1}t-\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}t-2\sqrt{t}{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}-\varepsilon^{2}}{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(2\left(\sqrt{1-\varepsilon}-\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)-\frac{2}{3}\left((1-\varepsilon)^{\frac{3}{2}}-\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\right)\right)-\frac{4}{3}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left((1-\varepsilon)^{\frac{3}{2}}-\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\right),

there exists a constant
\check{C}_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left(2V_{1}(\varepsilon)-E[J_{0,1}^{\varepsilon}]^{2}+\frac{8}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{2}{3}\frac{(1-\varepsilon)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)=\check{C}_{1}.

(A.4)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{3}V_{3}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\int_{t+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}u\int_{t}^{u-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{u}\text{\rm d}s(s-r)^{-\frac{3}{2}}(u-s+r-t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s(s-r)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\int_{0}^{r}\text{\rm d}t\int_{s}^{1}\text{\rm d}u(u-s+r-t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s(s-r)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\int_{0}^{r}\text{\rm d}t\left((r-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}-(1-s+r-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=4\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s(s-r)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left(\sqrt{r}-\sqrt{1-s+r}+\sqrt{1-s}\right).

Since

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s(s-r)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{r}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r2\sqrt{r}\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}-(1-r)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)=\frac{4}{3}\frac{(1-\varepsilon)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+C_{3,1}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s(s-r)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{1-s+r}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\left(2\left(\frac{\sqrt{1-\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}-\frac{\sqrt{r}}{\sqrt{1-r}}\right)-\int_{\varepsilon}^{1-r}\text{\rm d}\sigma\frac{1}{\sqrt{\sigma(1-\sigma)}}\right)

\displaystyle=2\frac{(1-\varepsilon)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+C_{3,2}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s(s-r)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{1-s}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s\sqrt{1-s}\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}-s^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{3}\frac{(1-\varepsilon)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+C_{3,3}(\varepsilon),

where
C_{3,i}(\varepsilon)
(
i=1,2,3
) converge to some constants as
\varepsilon\to 0
, there exists a constant
\check{C}_{3}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left(2V_{3}(\varepsilon)-\frac{8}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{2}{3}\frac{(1-\varepsilon)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)=\check{C}_{3}.

(A.5)

To calculate
V_{2}(\varepsilon)
, we first divide the integration as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\int_{t+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}u\int_{0}^{t}\text{\rm d}r\int_{(r+\varepsilon)\vee t}^{u}\text{\rm d}sF(r,s,t,u)

(A.6)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s\int_{r}^{s}\text{\rm d}t\int_{(t+\varepsilon)\vee s}^{1}\text{\rm d}uF(r,s,t,u)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s\int_{r}^{s-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\int_{s}^{1}\text{\rm d}uF(r,s,t,u)+\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s-\varepsilon}^{s}\text{\rm d}t\int_{t+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}uF(r,s,t,u),

where
F(r,s,t,u)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\left((t-r)(s-t)+(u-s)(s-t)+(u-s)(t-r)\right)^{-\frac{3}{2}}
is a continuous function on
\{0<r<t<s<u<1\}
.
In particular, we have

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{3}V_{2,1}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s\int_{r}^{s-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\int_{s}^{1}\text{\rm d}uF(r,s,t,u)

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s\int_{r}^{s-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t\frac{1}{s-r}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{(t-r)(s-t)}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{(1-s)(s-r)+(t-r)(s-t)}}\right)

and

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{3}V_{2,2}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s-\varepsilon}^{s}\text{\rm d}t\int_{t+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}uF(r,s,t,u)

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s-\varepsilon}^{s}\text{\rm d}t\frac{1}{s-r}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\times\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{(t+\varepsilon-s)(s-r)+(t-r)(s-t)}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{(1-s)(s-r)+(t-r)(s-t)}}\right).

Thus,

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{3}\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}(V_{2,1}(\varepsilon)+V_{2,2}(\varepsilon))

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s-\varepsilon}^{s}\text{\rm d}t\left((t+\varepsilon-s)(s-r)+(t-r)(s-t)\right)^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{r}^{r+\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{(t-r)\varepsilon+(t-r)(r+\varepsilon-t)}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{(1-r-\varepsilon)\varepsilon+(t-r)(r+\varepsilon-t)}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s-\varepsilon}^{s}\text{\rm d}t\int_{0}^{s-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\left((t+\varepsilon-s)(s-t)+\varepsilon(t-r)\right)^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}r\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\frac{\text{\rm d}u}{\sqrt{u(2\varepsilon-u)}}+O\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\int_{\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s-\varepsilon}^{s}\text{\rm d}t\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon^{2}-(s-t)^{2}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon s-(s-t)^{2}}}\right)-\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}(1-\varepsilon)+O\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon}(1-\varepsilon)+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\int_{\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}s\arcsin\sqrt{\frac{\varepsilon}{s}}=-\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon}+O\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Therefore, there exists a constant
\check{C}_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left(2V_{2}(\varepsilon)+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{2}}\log\varepsilon\right)=\check{C}_{2}.

(A.7)

(
A.4
)-(
A.7
) yield (
1.6
).

A.5.3
Proof of (
1.7
)

First, we remark that the proof (
1.7
) was already indicated in
[
47
, p.20]
but we shall give more detail by the following calculation.

As in (
A.6
), we have

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{3}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}u\int_{\varepsilon}^{u}\text{\rm d}t\frac{1}{(s(t-s)+(t-s)(u-t)+(u-t)s)^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle+\int_{\varepsilon}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}u\int_{s}^{u}\text{\rm d}t\frac{1}{(s(t-s)+(t-s)(u-t)+(u-t)s)^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

Hence,

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{3}\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\int_{\varepsilon}^{s+\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t(s(t-s)+(s+\varepsilon-t)t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle-\int_{\varepsilon}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t(s(t-s)+(s+\varepsilon-t)t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}u(s(\varepsilon-s)+(u-\varepsilon)\varepsilon)^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

Using the integral

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}(ax^{2}+bx+c)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\text{\rm d}x=\left[\frac{4ax+2b}{(4ac-b^{2})\sqrt{ax^{2}+bx+c}}\right]_{s}^{t},

we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\int_{\varepsilon}^{s+\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t(s(t-s)+(s+\varepsilon-t)t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\left(\frac{2}{(4s+\varepsilon)\sqrt{\varepsilon s}}-\frac{2}{\varepsilon\sqrt{2\varepsilon s-s^{2}}}+\frac{12s}{\varepsilon(4s+\varepsilon)\sqrt{2\varepsilon s-s^{2}}}\right),

\displaystyle\int_{\varepsilon}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}t(s(t-s)+(s+\varepsilon-t)t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\varepsilon}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\frac{4}{(4s+\varepsilon)\sqrt{\varepsilon s}},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\int_{s+\varepsilon}^{1}\text{\rm d}u(s(\varepsilon-s)+(u-\varepsilon)\varepsilon)^{-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\varepsilon s-s^{2}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon)+s(\varepsilon-s)}}\right).

Thus, we find that

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{3}\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\left(\frac{2}{(4s+\varepsilon)\sqrt{\varepsilon s}}+\frac{12\sqrt{s}}{\varepsilon(4s+\varepsilon)\sqrt{2\varepsilon-s}}\right)-\int_{\varepsilon}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\frac{4}{(4s+\varepsilon)\sqrt{\varepsilon s}}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon)+s(\varepsilon-s)}}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\frac{4}{(4s+\varepsilon)\sqrt{\varepsilon s}}+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\frac{2}{(4s+\varepsilon)\sqrt{\varepsilon s}}-\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\frac{12\sqrt{s}}{\varepsilon(4s+\varepsilon)\sqrt{2\varepsilon-s}}

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

It is easy to see from (
A.1
) that

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{1-\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\frac{4}{(4s+\varepsilon)\sqrt{\varepsilon s}}+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\frac{2}{(4s+\varepsilon)\sqrt{\varepsilon s}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4}{\varepsilon}\arctan\left(2\sqrt{\frac{1-\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}}\right)+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\arctan 2

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon}+O\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)+\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\arctan\frac{1}{2}.

Also, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}s\frac{12\sqrt{s}}{\varepsilon(4s+\varepsilon)\sqrt{2\varepsilon-s}}

\displaystyle=\frac{12}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{1}\text{\rm d}t\frac{\sqrt{t}}{(4t+1)\sqrt{2-t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{12}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{1}\text{\rm d}u\frac{4u^{2}}{(9u^{2}+1)(u^{2}+1)}

\displaystyle(u=\sqrt{\frac{t}{2-t}})

\displaystyle=\frac{3\pi}{2\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\arctan 3=\frac{\pi}{2\varepsilon}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\arctan\frac{1}{3}.

Since
\arctan\frac{1}{2}+\arctan\frac{1}{3}=\frac{\pi}{4}
,

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{3}\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)=-\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon}+O\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

(A.8)

We can also compute

\displaystyle\int_{s-\varepsilon^{\beta}}^{s}\text{\rm d}u\int_{s\vee(u+\varepsilon)}^{s+\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}v\frac{(2\pi)^{-3}}{((s+\varepsilon-v)(v-s)+(s+\varepsilon-v)(s-u)+(v-s)(s-u))^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon}\text{\rm d}v\left(\frac{2(2\pi)^{-3}}{\varepsilon((s+\varepsilon-v)(v+\varepsilon-s))^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{2(2\pi)^{-3}}{\varepsilon\left(\varepsilon^{\beta+1}+(s+\varepsilon-v)(v-s)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{-3}\pi}{\varepsilon}+O(\varepsilon^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2}})=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\text{\rm d}}{\text{\rm d}\varepsilon}\kappa_{2}(\varepsilon)+O(\varepsilon^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2}})

(A.9)

for
\varepsilon^{\alpha}<s<1-\varepsilon^{\alpha}
with
\alpha\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
and
\beta\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
.
